# Blueprint requirement catalogue

Generated by `qip blueprint render` from
`docs/blueprint/requirements/*.json`. **Do not edit this file by hand**:
edit the JSON and re-render. `qip blueprint check` fails when this view is stale.

Sources and precedence: [README.md](README.md). Architecture of record:
ADR 0099.

**1518 requirements** in 31 domains. Priority: P0 411, P1 521, P2 451, P3 135.

P0 covers safety and correctness invariants, contracts, and the exit criteria of the
foundation phases. P1 covers the cognitive data loop, the tick lake and replay,
multi-asset capital and risk, and multi-region. P2 covers arbitrage, market making,
quantum, prediction and commerce, and AGI autonomy. P3 covers open-ended expansion
and causal agency.

## Policy flags

A flag marks a requirement that cannot be fully met without something a standing
decision refuses. The conflict register is in ADR 0099.

| Flag | Requirements | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `LIVE_CAPITAL` | 17 | needs live orders or real money movement; refused (ADR 0003/0021, conflict C1) |
| `EXTERNAL_ACTION` | 84 | needs the platform to act on the outside world; refused (C1) |
| `NEW_DEPENDENCY` | 25 | needs a crate beyond serde/serde_json (C2) |
| `MANAGED_SERVICE` | 149 | needs a GCP managed data/AI service (C4) |
| `KUBERNETES` | 71 | needs GKE / Argo / service mesh (C3) |
| `NON_RUST` | 9 | needs a non-Rust runtime (C5) |
| `MULTI_REPO` | 8 | needs a multi-repository split (C6, declined) |
| `MULTI_REGION` | 71 | needs more than one region (C8) |
| `COST` | 54 | implies significant recurring spend (C8) |

## ARCH

Architecture: time lanes, planes, cross-cutting invariants, build-order phases.

| ID | P | Kind | Requirement | Flags | Verification | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R12-ARCH-8001 | P0 | invariant | **Cognition stays asynchronous to live order execution** — The Superintelligence/cognition plane (latent state, forecasts, agents, twins, routing) must remain asynchronous to live order execution: no execution-path crate may depend on a cognition crate, and the fastest path stays regional and deterministic. |  | acceptance: A dependency-graph walk from every edge/reflex crate finds no cognition, language-model or solver crate; adding such an edge to qip-edge's Cargo.toml must fail the test. | GCP v2.1 p2 §1 Executive Architecture |
| R12-ARCH-8021 | P1 | invariant | **Durable async work runs after or beside the reflex decision** — Control, outcomes, journals and telemetry must be durable where required but executed after or beside the reflex decision, never in front of it. |  | integration: A stalled journal destination does not delay a pass; removing the async boundary must fail the test. | GCP v2.1 p3 §2 Non-Negotiable Architecture Rules |
| R12-ARCH-8028 | P0 | invariant | **Caches own nothing authoritative** — Caches (including Memorystore Redis) must never hold authoritative state; loss of a cache causes rebuild or degraded latency, never lost financial state. |  | acceptance: Every composition root resolving a storage target refuses a cache as store of record; resolving Memorystore as key_value must fail. | GCP v2.1 p3 §2 Non-Negotiable Architecture Rules; GCP v2.1 p10 §9.2 Durable Knowledge Tiers |
| R12-ARCH-8029 | P1 | invariant | **Explicit state ownership across Spanner, Fabric, GCS, Bigtable** — State ownership must be explicit: Spanner owns financial truth; the Fabric owns event history/offsets; GCS owns retained archives and derived knowledge artifacts; Bigtable owns hot derived features; external payloads are transient. |  | inspection: Each fact class maps to one store in code; a second writer of the same class must fail an architecture test. | GCP v2.1 p3 §2 Non-Negotiable Architecture Rules |
| R12-ARCH-9012 | P2 | constraint | **Intelligence decomposed into independently scalable services** — The intelligence platform is decomposed into independently scalable services rather than one global model endpoint. |  | inspection: Each named cognitive service is a separately deployable binary with its own scaling settings. | GCP v2.1 p11 §11.1 Superintelligence Service Topology |
| R12-ARCH-9014 | P0 | invariant | **Each cognitive service degrades without stopping local execution** — Every cognitive service can degrade or fail without stopping local (Reflex) execution. |  | integration: With every central input stale/unavailable the cell still completes passes with reduced sizing; making a capability required for a pass fails the test. | GCP v2.1 p11 §11.1 Superintelligence Service Topology |
| R12-ARCH-9028 | P3 | constraint | **Agents are not all required to use the same runtime** — The platform must not require every agent to use the same runtime; managed sessions/memory may use Agent Engine and long-lived deterministic agents run elsewhere. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | inspection: An agent manifest can name its runtime and two runtimes are supported. | GCP v2.1 p12 §11.4 Predictive Model & Agent Society |
| R12-ARCH-9071 | P0 | constraint | **Architecture agent cannot silently redefine non-negotiables** — Architecture checks enforce dependency rules, hot-path violations and state ownership, and non-negotiables cannot be redefined silently. |  | inspection: The boundary is enforced by credentials/config or a gate, and a run attempting the forbidden action is refused. | GCP v2.1 p15 §13 Autonomous Development and AI Engineering |
| R12-ARCH-10061 | P1 | process | **Every cloud service has a reason, connections, scaling and degraded mode** — Every cloud service/workload added has one primary reason, known upstream/downstream connections, a scaling model and a degraded-mode contract, with a documented state owner; services without a distinct capability are not added. |  | inspection: Each catalogue entry carries owner, connections, scaling and degraded-mode fields and a test refuses an entry missing one. | GCP v2.1 p21 §19.0A Service-by-Service Production Architecture; GCP v2.1 p28 §27 Architecture Acceptance Criteria |
| R12-ARCH-10159 | P1 | process | **Build phase 0: Foundations** — Phase 0 (Foundations) delivers: org/folders/projects, IAM/PAM, Terraform modules, NCC/VPC/DNS/firewalls, KMS/CA, audit/SCC, GitHub WIF, meeting its exit test before later phases depend on it. |  | inspection: Each listed deliverable exists and its exit test passes. | GCP v2.1 p27 §26 Recommended Build Order |
| R12-ARCH-10176 | P0 | invariant | **No synchronous remote call between venue event and local order** — No synchronous call to Fabric, warm services, Spanner, Bigtable, Redis, BigQuery, AI or another region is required between a venue event and a local order. |  | integration: With every remote dependency stalled, a venue event still produces an order in bounded time; inserting a blocking remote call before the pass fails. | GCP v2.1 p28 §27 Architecture Acceptance Criteria |
| R12-ARCH-10193 | P0 | invariant | **No superintelligence service is a synchronous dependency between tick and order** — No agent, world model, forecast service, TPU job or QPU workflow becomes a synchronous dependency between tick and order. |  | integration: Edge node depends on no cognitive crate and trades identically with the centre silent. | GCP v2.1 p29 §27.1 Superintelligence Acceptance Criteria; GCP v2.1 p30 §31 Final Platform Definition |
| R12-ARCH-1001 | P1 | invariant | **Federated cognition: global core, specialist brains, regional reflex** — Intelligence is organised as a federation of a global cognitive core, specialist brains and regional reflex intelligence, not one central process that runs once. | `MULTI_REGION` | inspection: Composition roots exist for a central plane (qip-api/qip-fastbrain/qip-deepbrain) and a regional reflex binary (qip-edge-node) with no compile-time dependency between them; failing mutation: add qip-kernel to qip-edge-node's dependencies. | v11.6 p2 §1 Executive Summary; v11.6 p4 §2 Corrections Required to v10.1 |
| R12-ARCH-1008 | P1 | capability | **Warm coordination layer between slow and fast systems** — A warm coordination layer sits between the slow cognitive and fast reflex systems for cross-region arbitrage, capital movement, portfolio management, market creation and settlement. |  | integration: A central component ingests cell reports and re-issues capital envelopes on a seconds-to-minutes cadence; removing ingestion leaves envelopes un-renewed. | v11.6 p2 §1 Executive Summary |
| R12-ARCH-1070 | P1 | invariant | **Global state authoritative long-horizon; regions keep local state** — Global state is authoritative for long-horizon knowledge; regions retain local state and can peer-share fast state. |  | integration: A cell keeps its own ledger and state across a centre outage, and long-horizon knowledge is only written centrally. | v11.6 p4 §2 Corrections Required to v10.1 |
| R12-ARCH-1185 | P1 | constraint | **Each function lives in the fastest lane meeting its correctness** — Every function is assigned to exactly one time lane: the fastest lane that can meet its correctness requirements. |  | inspection: Every workspace crate has exactly one lane and a stated requirement, and no faster-lane crate depends on a slower one. | v11.6 p9 §4 Time-Lane Architecture |
| R12-ARCH-1191 | P1 | capability | **Lane 2 tactical/warm responsibilities** — Lane 2 (seconds to minutes) carries portfolio/risk refresh, capital placement, treasury moves, market-making regime shifts, event repricing, cross-region inventory rebalancing, aggregated microstructure state, liquidity maps, execution-quality refresh, short-horizon fusion and bounded tactical interventions. |  | integration: The central cycle refreshes portfolio/risk and capital placement on a minute cadence. | v11.6 p9 §4 Time-Lane Architecture |
| R12-ARCH-1192 | P2 | capability | **Lane 3 cognitive slow responsibilities** — Lane 3 (minutes to days) hosts discovery, evidence synthesis, causal reasoning, research, hypothesis generation, simulation, strategy discovery, training/validation, tick replay, microstructure training, ambient models, symbolic reasoners, arbitration and intervention planning. |  | inspection: Lane-3 crates are registered lane 3 and composed in qip-deepbrain. | v11.6 p10 §4 Time-Lane Architecture |
| R12-ARCH-3001 | P0 | invariant | **Cognitive Core stays off the hot order path** — The Cognitive Core (slow-thinking planner/scientific engine) must not act as the hot-path trader: no cognitive-core crate or language model is reachable from the reflex cell's order path. |  | acceptance: Dependency-graph test fails if qip-edge or qip-edge-node can reach qip-ai/qip-reasoning-engine/qip-world-model or a language-model provider; adding such a dependency must turn it red. | v11.6 p15 §9 Financial Superintelligence / Cognitive Core |
| R12-ARCH-7134 | P1 | process | **Architecture complete; implementation gated by phase exits** — Implementation proceeds in gated phases; a phase's exit capability is demonstrated before the next phase's capabilities are enabled. |  | inspection: Each phase has a recorded exit check; later-phase features stay disabled until it passes. | v11.6 p47 §30 Build Order |
| R12-ARCH-7135 | P1 | process | **Phase 0 exit: contracts, ledger, simulation, observability, CI/CD, identity, venue SDK** — Phase 0 exits with canonical types/contracts, ledger, simulation harness, observability, CI/CD/GitOps, identity/security and a venue adapter SDK. |  | inspection: Each named foundation exists and is tested. | v11.6 p47 §30 Build Order |
| R12-ARCH-7139 | P1 | process | **Phase 2 exit: cognitive data loop** — Phase 2 exits with Scout Fabric, evidence/provenance, world model, episodic memory, Tick Learning Fabric, Tick Lake, replay/digital twin, world+market fusion, model/strategy foundry, initial World Model Federation, disagreement representation and symbolic/temporal/causal reasoning services. |  | inspection: Every named exit capability exists and is tested. | v11.6 p47 §30 Build Order |
| R12-ARCH-7140 | P1 | process | **Phase 3 exit: multi-asset with capital and risk brains** — Phase 3 exits with asset registry, Asset/Capital/Risk brains, treasury, custody corridors and multiple asset classes. |  | inspection: Every named exit capability exists and is tested. | v11.6 p47 §30 Build Order |

## CONTRACT

Typed contracts between brains and components.

| ID | P | Kind | Requirement | Flags | Verification | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R12-CONTRACT-9013 | P1 | contract | **Cognitive services exchange typed Rust Fabric contracts and own narrow state** — Each cognitive service publishes/consumes typed Rust Fabric contracts and owns a narrow state domain. |  | unit: Every cross-service message is a typed qip-contracts body with a schema-lock row; a service reading another's state directly fails the architecture suite. | GCP v2.1 p11 §11.1 Superintelligence Service Topology |
| R12-CONTRACT-9121 | P0 | process | **Schema changes: compatibility gate, additive first, dual read/write, retire after inventory** — Schemas live in a registry with a CI compatibility gate; changes are additive first with dual-read/write migrations, and a field is retired only after a consumer inventory proves it safe. |  | acceptance: Removing a field fails the gate; adding passes. | GCP v2.1 p16 §14.3 Component-specific release mechanics |
| R12-CONTRACT-1195 | P0 | contract | **Signed model/strategy/belief/capital/risk packs flow to the reflex mesh** — Foundries and brains publish signed model, strategy, belief, capital and risk packs down to regional reflex cells. |  | integration: A cell installs only packs whose signature verifies; tampered packs are refused. | v11.6 p11 §5 End-to-End System Path |
| R12-CONTRACT-3011 | P1 | contract | **Cognitive Core produces deployable policy/model packages** — The Cognitive Core's output is a signed, versioned policy or model package that the warm/reflex lanes can consume. |  | integration: The central plane issues a signed policy payload a cell accepts; tampering is refused. | v11.6 p15 §9 Financial Superintelligence / Cognitive Core |
| R12-CONTRACT-3117 | P1 | contract | **Typed Forecast contract for every material prediction** — Every material prediction uses a typed Forecast contract containing outcome distribution, horizon, target variable, epistemic uncertainty, aleatoric uncertainty, tail mass, model disagreement, regime confidence, expected impact, evidence lineage and expiration. |  | unit: A Forecast missing any field is refused on decode; every material prediction site constructs one. | v11.6 p20 §9.14 Forecast Contract, Calibration & Uncertainty Decomposition |

## FABRIC

Native Rust Event & Control Fabric.

| R12-FABRIC-8004 | P1 | capability | **Fabric runs on dedicated Rust broker VMs per region** — The Rust Event & Control Fabric must run as dedicated Rust broker VMs in each region, providing one native contract for durable pub/sub, journals, replay, backpressure, control and outcomes. | `MULTI_REGION` | plan: Each production region's plan contains broker instances running qip-fabricd; removing the module instantiation must fail the infrastructure acceptance test. | GCP v2.1 p2 §1 Executive Architecture |
| R12-FABRIC-8009 | P1 | invariant | **No Pub/Sub or Managed Kafka in the internal runtime** — Google Pub/Sub and Managed Kafka must not be internal runtime dependencies for service-to-service control, tick journaling, outcomes or replay. |  | acceptance: No workspace crate declares a Pub/Sub/Kafka client and no Terraform grants publish/subscribe on a fabric topic; adding either must fail the scan. | GCP v2.1 p2 §1 Executive Architecture |

| R12-FABRIC-8022 | P0 | contract | **One internal event model with mandatory envelope fields** — Every asynchronous event must carry event_id, producer_epoch, schema_id/version, region, logical timestamp, source timestamp, trace_id, ordering key, integrity checksum and provenance metadata. |  | unit: Encoding then decoding an event preserves all ten fields; building one with any field empty is refused. | GCP v2.1 p3 §2 Non-Negotiable Architecture Rules |
| R12-FABRIC-8023 | P0 | invariant | **At-least-once with idempotency; no exactly-once claim** — The platform must not claim global exactly-once delivery; consumers must deduplicate using event IDs, partition sequence and fencing epochs. |  | property: Under random lost acks, retries and restarts each sequence is appended once; a consumer replaying from an offset produces no duplicate effect. | GCP v2.1 p3 §2 Non-Negotiable Architecture Rules |

| R12-FABRIC-8074 | P1 | constraint | **Broker uses bounded memory** — The broker must use bounded memory pools for requests and buffers. |  | integration: A request over the bound is refused; concurrent connections are capped. | GCP v2.1 p7 §7 Algorik Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-8075 | P1 | capability | **fabric-meta: replicated metadata quorum separate from data log** — Cluster membership, partition maps, epochs, consumer leases and quotas must be held in a small replicated (Raft) metadata quorum, separated from the data log. | `NEW_DEPENDENCY` | property: Metadata survives loss of a minority of meta nodes; partition leadership is assigned from it. | GCP v2.1 p7 §7 Algorik Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-8076 | P1 | capability | **fabric-client SDK: idempotent producer, batching, ack profile** — The producer/consumer SDK must provide an idempotent producer sequence, batching and an explicit acknowledgement profile. |  | integration: SDK producer against fabricd never duplicates on retry and reports quorum only when durable. | GCP v2.1 p7 §7 Algorik Rust Event & Control Fabric |

| R12-FABRIC-8078 | P1 | capability | **fabric-mirror: selective inter-region replication** — fabric-mirror must replicate only configured topics/partitions between regions, with an independent lag budget, never blocking local commit. | `MULTI_REGION` | integration: A mirrored partition appears in the peer region; stopping the peer does not delay local produce. | GCP v2.1 p7 §7 Algorik Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-8079 | P1 | capability | **fabric-archive: immutable segments with manifest, hydrate replay** — Closed immutable segments must be archived with manifest/checksum, and replay must be able to hydrate from the archive. |  | integration: An archived segment is read back byte-identically; a tampered archive object is refused. | GCP v2.1 p7 §7 Algorik Rust Event & Control Fabric |

| R12-FABRIC-8084 | P0 | contract | **Fabric frame header fields with CRC32C** — Each frame header must carry magic/version, message type, schema ID/version, flags, partition, producer ID/epoch, sequence, event ID, source timestamp, logical timestamp, trace ID, payload length and CRC32C. |  | unit: Round-trip preserves every field; a bit flip anywhere is detected as corruption. | GCP v2.1 p8 §7.1 Wire, ordering and delivery semantics |
| R12-FABRIC-8085 | P1 | capability | **Explicit ordering key, rendezvous leaders, cross-zone followers** — Records must be partitioned by an explicit ordering key, partitions assigned to leaders by rendezvous/consistent hashing, and leaders replicate to followers in other zones. |  | property: Same key → same partition in produce order; leader assignment stable under broker list order; follower holds every committed batch. | GCP v2.1 p8 §7.1 Wire, ordering and delivery semantics |

| R12-FABRIC-8087 | P0 | invariant | **At-least-once delivery, producer fencing, per-partition replay** — Delivery to consumers must be at-least-once; producer epoch/sequence must prevent stale duplicate producers; per-partition offsets must permit replay. |  | property: Superseded producer never appends after successor's first append; replay from any offset returns identical batches. | GCP v2.1 p8 §7.1 Wire, ordering and delivery semantics |
| R12-FABRIC-8088 | P1 | constraint | **Atomic batches within a partition; sagas across partitions** — Append batches must be atomic within one partition, and cross-partition business transactions must be sagas/commands rather than broker transactions. |  | property: A segment cut at any byte recovers whole batches only; no API appends to two partitions atomically. | GCP v2.1 p8 §7.1 Wire, ordering and delivery semantics |

| R12-FABRIC-7034 | P1 | capability | **Brokers enforce per-stream retention** — Brokers apply each stream's retention policy. |  | integration: Segments older than the stream's retention are removed after archival. | v11.6 p41 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7035 | P1 | capability | **Partition leaders/followers with replication** — Brokers act as regional partition leaders/followers and replicate partition logs. |  | integration: A follower holds an identical log; killing the leader loses no committed record. | v11.6 p41 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7036 | P1 | capability | **Brokers apply backpressure** — Brokers apply backpressure to producers that exceed quotas or lag limits. |  | integration: A producer over quota is refused/slowed and the refusal is visible. | v11.6 p41 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7037 | P1 | capability | **fabric-controller three-zone Raft metadata quorum** — A fabric-controller runs a three-zone metadata quorum (Rust Raft) for stream definitions, partition placement, broker epochs, ACL metadata and leadership, with no cloud database required for regional operation. | `MULTI_REGION` | game-day: Losing one of three controller nodes keeps metadata writable; losing two halts control changes only. | v11.6 p41 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7038 | P1 | capability | **fabric-mirror selective async cross-region replication** — fabric-mirror replicates selected streams asynchronously cross-region with no global write quorum; each region stays independently writable. | `MULTI_REGION` | integration: Mirror copies selected streams; local writes succeed while the mirror is down. | v11.6 p41 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7039 | P1 | capability | **fabric-schema: versioned descriptors, compatibility checks, schema hashes** — fabric-schema maintains versioned contract descriptors, compatibility checks, schema hashes and generated Rust types. |  | unit: Removing/renaming a field without a version bump is refused; identical content yields identical schema id. | v11.6 p41 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7040 | P1 | capability | **fabric-archive exports sealed segments to Cloud Storage** — Sealed segments are exported to Cloud Storage/Tick Lake with manifests, hashes, entitlements and replay indexes. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | integration: Every sealed segment of an archive-required stream lands once in the archive with manifest and hash. | v11.6 p41 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7041 | P2 | constraint | **fabric-bridge adapters are optional and edge-only** — External Kafka/Pub/Sub/webhook bridges are optional edge-only adapters; internal services use the native protocol. |  | acceptance: Only a bridge crate may hold a client, it holds no durable state, and nothing depends on it. | v11.6 p41 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7042 | P1 | capability | **fabricctl operator CLI for health, lag, ACLs, mirroring, replay, isolation** — A Rust operator CLI reports stream/partition health, lag, ACLs, mirroring and replay and performs emergency isolation. |  | integration: CLI isolates a partition and the broker refuses produce until release. | v11.6 p41 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7043 | P1 | capability | **fabric-bench latency/throughput benchmark harness** — A latency/throughput benchmark harness exists for the fabric. |  | inspection: A bench target reports produce/fetch latency percentiles. | v11.6 p41 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7044 | P1 | process | **fabric-chaos deterministic fault/partition testing in CI and game days** — Deterministic fault/partition testing of the fabric runs in CI and game days. |  | game-day: A seeded fault test kills/partitions brokers and asserts no committed loss. | v11.6 p41 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7045 | P1 | capability | **Rust SDK: idempotent sequencing, groups, async subscriptions, OTel hooks** — The Rust SDK offers zero-copy-friendly producer/consumer APIs, idempotent producer sequencing, consumer groups, async subscriptions, local ring-buffer integration and OpenTelemetry hooks. |  | integration: SDK producer and served broker agree on sequences; duplicate producer sequence is deduplicated. | v11.6 p41 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7046 | P1 | constraint | **QUIC over UDP with mutual TLS for broker, client and mirror traffic** — Broker/client and cross-region mirror traffic uses QUIC over UDP with mutual TLS, with long-lived streams for durable records. | `NEW_DEPENDENCY` | integration: A client without a valid certificate cannot connect; traffic is QUIC. | v11.6 p42 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7047 | P1 | invariant | **QUIC datagrams only for explicitly lossy telemetry classes** — Unreliable datagrams are used only for streams explicitly declared lossy telemetry. |  | unit: A durable class configured for datagram delivery is refused. | v11.6 p42 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7048 | P2 | constraint | **Wire format is versioned Protobuf via prost** — Contracts are encoded on the wire as versioned Protobuf via prost for compatibility. | `NEW_DEPENDENCY` | unit: Encoded records decode with prost against the registered descriptor. | v11.6 p42 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7049 | P0 | invariant | **Records and segments carry BLAKE3/content hashes and CRC** — Every record/segment carries a content hash (BLAKE3) and CRC protection. |  | unit: A flipped bit is refused as corruption naming its offset. | v11.6 p42 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7050 | P1 | invariant | **No unversioned in-memory layouts on the network** — Hot in-process data structures may use zero-copy layouts but never leak unversioned memory formats onto the network. |  | unit: Every encoded batch carries magic/version; a wrong version is refused. | v11.6 p42 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7051 | P0 | invariant | **Strict per-partition order; no global total order promised** — Ordering is strict within a partition and no total global order is promised. |  | property: Random produce/fetch/restart keeps offsets dense and fetches in append order. | v11.6 p42 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7052 | P0 | contract | **Every record carries times, region, partition, epoch, offset, HLC** — Every record carries source event time, receive time, region, partition, leader epoch, offset and hybrid logical clock metadata. |  | unit: An envelope missing any of these fields is refused. | v11.6 p42 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7053 | P0 | invariant | **At-least-once default; at-most-once only telemetry; effectively-once via idempotency** — Durable streams default to at-least-once; at-most-once is allowed only for telemetry; idempotent producer ids/sequence numbers and deterministic event ids give effectively-once with idempotent sinks. |  | integration: A durable stream with an at-most-once profile is refused; a replayed producer sequence is not appended twice. | v11.6 p42 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7054 | P0 | constraint | **Durability classes: control quorum+WAL, outcomes quorum+archive, journals, telemetry** — Control streams use three-zone quorum plus persistent metadata/WAL; financial outcomes use quorum replication and archival; market journals use high-throughput replicated segments; telemetry can be sampled/dropped. |  | integration: Each class's declared ack/replication is what the broker enforces. | v11.6 p42 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7055 | P1 | contract | **Every stream has byte/message quotas, lag limits and overload policy** — Every stream declares explicit byte/message quotas, lag limits and an overload policy. |  | unit: A stream missing a quota, lag limit or overload policy is refused. | v11.6 p42 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7056 | P0 | invariant | **Critical control/outcomes never silently dropped; telemetry may be shed** — Critical control and outcome records cannot be silently dropped; telemetry and low-value ambient signals may be sampled or shed. |  | integration: Under overload, P0/P1 producers are refused (visible) not dropped; a recorded-inputs overflow writes a gap on P1. | v11.6 p42 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7057 | P0 | invariant | **Cross-region mirroring is async; regional cluster never blocks on remote ack** — Selected streams mirror asynchronously over Google private networking and a regional cluster never blocks on remote acknowledgement. | `MULTI_REGION` | game-day: Cutting the inter-region link does not block local produce. | v11.6 p42 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7058 | P0 | invariant | **Reflex node publishes via non-blocking local ring/journal writer on a separate core** — The Reflex Node publishes through a non-blocking local ring/journal writer running on a separate core. |  | integration: Decision thread hands off with try_send; a stalled writer leaves entries unshipped without blocking. | v11.6 p42 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7060 | P1 | contract | **Packages are signed immutable artifacts; fabric carries only digests/activation** — Model and policy packages are immutable signed artifacts in object/artifact storage; the fabric carries only version/digest/availability/activation messages. |  | unit: A model manifest carries only digests and is size-bounded; unknown fields refused. | v11.6 p42 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-FABRIC-7136 | P1 | process | **Phase 0 includes fabric contracts, 3-zone quorum, SDK, benchmark harness** — Native fabric contracts, 3-zone regional quorum, Rust SDK and benchmark harness are delivered in Phase 0. |  | inspection: All four exist and are tested. | v11.6 p47 §30 Build Order |
| R12-FABRIC-7142 | P1 | constraint | **Phase 4: selective mirroring for outcomes/control; arbitrage on direct mesh** — Phase 4 adds selective cross-region fabric mirroring for outcomes/control while latency-sensitive arbitrage state stays on direct Reflex Mesh links. | `MULTI_REGION` | inspection: Mirror catalogue lists only outcome/control streams; arbitrage uses mesh links. | v11.6 p47 §30 Build Order |
| R12-FABRIC-7211 | P1 | capability | **Fabric is regionally independent, replayable, backpressure-aware, selectively mirrored** — The fabric carries control, journals and outcomes and is regionally independent, replayable, backpressure-aware and selectively mirrored. | `MULTI_REGION` | integration: Each property demonstrated by a fabricd test. | v11.6 p51 §33 Final Definition of Algorik |

Regional Reflex Cell / Node (hot path).

| R12-REFLEX-8002 | P1 | constraint | **Reflex plane on dedicated C4D/C4 Compute Engine VMs** — Reflex Execution must run on dedicated Compute Engine C4D/C4-class VMs with direct venue sessions, local books/features/models/risk, and no service-mesh or database dependency. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | plan: terraform plan with a declared execution node shows a google_compute_instance_group_manager on a c4/c4d shape with no load balancer or mesh; a C3 or N2 shape must be refused at plan. | GCP v2.1 p2 §1 Executive Architecture |
| R12-REFLEX-8020 | P0 | invariant | **Hot path owns its dependencies: tick to order is local only** — Between tick and order only local process memory, local deterministic risk, local session state and the venue adapter may be used — no GKE, Spanner, Bigtable, Redis, AI endpoint or fabric acknowledgement. |  | acceptance: Dependency-graph tests show no edge crate reaches storage, LM, solver or transport for lane-0 stages; adding qip-storage to qip-edge must fail. | GCP v2.1 p3 §2 Non-Negotiable Architecture Rules |
| R12-REFLEX-8063 | P1 | constraint | **Reflex compute: C4D/C4, gVNIC/Tier-1, reservations** — Reflex VMs must be C4D/C4-class sized by venue/instrument shard, use gVNIC/Tier-1 networking where benchmarked beneficial, and use capacity reservations in production. | `COST` | plan: Plan shows c4/c4d, GVNIC, TIER_1 and reservation affinity. | GCP v2.1 p6 §6 Regional Reflex Cell |
| R12-REFLEX-8064 | P1 | constraint | **Reflex binary under systemd on hardened image, no Kubernetes** — The Rust Reflex binary must run directly under systemd on a hardened minimal image, with no Kubernetes or service mesh in the decision loop. |  | acceptance: Node template has no container runtime; boot image bake installs exactly the required binaries; shielded VM on. | GCP v2.1 p6 §6 Regional Reflex Cell |
| R12-REFLEX-8065 | P1 | constraint | **OS tuning: isolated CPUs, huge pages, mlockall, IRQ/RPS/XPS** — Reflex hosts must apply isolated CPU sets, CPU affinity, huge pages, mlockall, tuned IRQ/RPS/XPS, bounded background services, local scratch SSD and deterministic allocator choices. |  | acceptance: Startup refuses a host lacking isolcpus/hugepages/no-swap; the binary locks memory; IRQ affinity is set. | GCP v2.1 p6 §6 Regional Reflex Cell |

| R12-REFLEX-8069 | P0 | invariant | **Fenced warm standby; never two active owners** — A fenced warm standby must run in another zone; activation requires liveness failure plus fencing-epoch and venue-session ownership checks, so two active owners never exist. |  | property: For any interleaving of primary/standby liveness, at most one holds the fencing epoch; removing the epoch check must fail. | GCP v2.1 p6 §6 Regional Reflex Cell |
| R12-REFLEX-8070 | P1 | capability | **Signed bundle package manager with staged epoch activation** — A package manager must download signed model/policy bundles outside the hot loop, verify manifest/KMS signature, stage, health-check, then atomically activate at an epoch boundary. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | integration: A tampered bundle is refused; a valid one activates only at a pass boundary; activation is atomic. | GCP v2.1 p6 §6 Regional Reflex Cell |
| R12-REFLEX-8071 | P1 | capability | **Instant local rollback of activated bundles** — The cell must retain the previous bundle locally and roll back instantly without a fetch. |  | integration: A rollback restores the previous payload without a fetch and is reachable from an operator/control path. | GCP v2.1 p6 §6 Regional Reflex Cell |
| R12-REFLEX-8072 | P0 | invariant | **Degraded mode narrows exposure then halts on freshness expiry** — On loss of Fabric/service plane/global AI the cell must continue under its last valid envelope, progressively narrow exposure per local policy, and exit/halt when freshness limits expire. |  | integration: Cut-off cell's order size falls a step per aged input and stops at envelope expiry with a named refusal. | GCP v2.1 p6 §6 Regional Reflex Cell |
| R12-REFLEX-9010 | P0 | invariant | **Reflex Node never calls a general model endpoint for a live order** — A Reflex Node never calls a general model endpoint to decide an order. |  | inspection: The dependency graph shows no edge crate or edge-node reaching the language-model/AI crates; adding such an edge fails the architecture suite. | GCP v2.1 p11 §11 Model-package rule |
| R12-REFLEX-9011 | P0 | invariant | **Reflex executes only locally loaded, tested, signed packages with deterministic risk** — A Reflex Node executes only a locally loaded, tested, signed model/policy package, with deterministic risk checks. |  | integration: The cell refuses a policy whose signature fails and a model its manifest does not name; risk gates are deterministic. | R12-REFLEX-5043 | P1 | capability | **Cross-strategy intent netting and internal crossing** — Intents from different strategies are netted and opposing interest is crossed internally before reaching a venue, with attribution preserved. |  | integration: Two opposing intents within the window cross at the mid and move both lots; mutation: disable cross_internally and both reach the venue. | v11.6 p29 §16 Regional Reflex Nodes (module table) |
| R12-REFLEX-5047 | P0 | capability | **Journal forwarded to ledger and learning asynchronously** — The node's durable local event stream is forwarded asynchronously to the ledger and to learning. |  | integration: A fill journaled at the node is booked by qip-ledgerd; mutation: drop the forward and the balance does not move. | v11.6 p29 §16 Regional Reflex Nodes (module table) |
| R12-REFLEX-6012 | P2 | capability | **Regional reflex episodes become globally shareable** — Microstructure drift, local failures, missed fills, queue behaviour and venue anomalies at a regional cell become globally shareable episodes. | `MULTI_REGION` | integration: An episode recorded at a cell reaches the central learning store; a cell-only buffer fails the test. | v11.6 p32 §22 Continuous Learning & Evaluation Brain |
| R12-REFLEX-6075 | P1 | constraint | **Reflex artifacts meet deterministic resource, latency, failure budgets** — A new reflex artifact must meet deterministic resource, latency and failure-mode budgets before regional deployment. | `MULTI_REGION` | unit: A student whose step cost, memory or failure-mode budget is exceeded is refused before it can be shipped to a cell. | v11.6 p36 §23.4 Controlled Self-Improvement and Promotion |
| R12-REFLEX-7003 | P2 | capability | **CPU pinning, huge pages, mlock, busy polling as justified** — Reflex nodes apply CPU pinning, huge pages, mlock and busy polling where measurement justifies them. |  | inspection: Node boot refuses without isolcpus/huge pages; process is pinned and memory-locked; removing isolcpus check lets an unpinned node boot. | v11.6 p39 §26 Platform & Deployment Architecture |
| R12-REFLEX-7059 | P0 | invariant | **Book, inference, risk veto and venue send never wait on broker ack** — Order-book processing, model inference, risk veto and venue send never wait on a broker acknowledgement. |  | acceptance: No edge crate depends on qip-streaming/transport broker client in the pass. | v11.6 p42 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-REFLEX-7061 | P0 | invariant | **Fabric outage: node continues on last valid package until TTL/risk degradation** — If the fabric is unavailable a node continues under its last valid package and local journal until the package TTL or risk policy requires degradation. |  | integration: With no new payload, the cell keeps trading at narrowing size and refuses after valid_for elapses. | v11.6 p42 §26.1 Native Rust Event & Control Fabric |
| R12-REFLEX-7137 | P0 | process | **Phase 1 exit: one reflex cell, paper, deterministic risk, ledger, replay** — Phase 1 exits with one asset/venue reflex cell: local fast path, deterministic risk, ledger, paper trading and replay. |  | integration: qip-edge-node runs passes on the simulated feed with deterministic risk and journal replay. | v11.6 p47 §30 Build Order |

| R12-CAPITAL-4088 | P0 | invariant | **Expiring strategy grant with exposure, liquidity, leverage, drawdown, loss budget** — Every strategy receives an expiring capital grant constrained by exposure, liquidity, leverage, drawdown and loss budget. |  | integration: The grant carries all five bounds and the cell refuses an order breaching any. | v11.6 p27 §13.2 Capital Survival Kernel |
| R12-CAPITAL-4089 | P0 | invariant | **Hedge capacity and stressed liquidity withheld before opportunity** — Capital for hedge capacity and stressed liquidity is withheld before opportunity capital is released. |  | integration: The construction budget is opportunity capital after hedge and stressed-liquidity reserves; removing the reserve raises the budget in the test. | v11.6 p27 §13.2 Capital Survival Kernel |
| R12-CAPITAL-4090 | P2 | capability | **Funding and collateral optimised across currencies, venues, entities** — Funding and collateral are optimised across currencies, venues, counterparties and legal entities while ledger truth remains authoritative. | `LIVE_CAPITAL` | integration: An optimiser proposes placements whose totals reconcile to the ledger. | v11.6 p27 §13.2 Capital Survival Kernel |
| R12-CAPITAL-4091 | P1 | capability | **Shadow portfolios for every real allocation** — For every real allocation the platform keeps counterfactual shadow portfolios with alternate sizes, hedge structures, timing, venues and rejected opportunities. |  | integration: Every placed and every declined order has priced alternatives for size, timing, venue and hedge; a declined order's 'trade' alternative prices the veto. | v11.6 p27 §13.3 Shadow Portfolio Universe |
| R12-CAPITAL-4092 | P1 | capability | **Separate prediction quality from sizing, execution and allocation quality** — Learning separates prediction quality from sizing quality, execution quality and capital-allocation quality. |  | integration: Per decision, a decomposition attributes outcome to prediction, sizing, execution and allocation components that sum to the total. | v11.6 p27 §13.3 Shadow Portfolio Universe |
| R12-CAPITAL-5067 | P1 | process | **Inventory drift becomes a rebalancing task** — Inventory drift after a cycle becomes a treasury/capital rebalancing task. |  | unit: Drift beyond tolerance yields one task per place citing the epoch; mutation: ignore tolerance and the test fails. | v11.6 p30 §17.2 Cross-Region Execution Protocol |
| R12-CAPITAL-5078 | P0 | invariant | **Market making and arbitrage share one reservation fabric** — Capital and inventory used by market making and arbitrage are reserved through one reservation fabric so neither can spend what the other holds. |  | integration: A quote and an arbitrage leg drawing on the same capital cannot both reserve it; mutation: give quoting a separate ledger and both pass. | v11.6 p30 §18.1 Market Making |
| R12-CAPITAL-7204 | P0 | invariant | **Capital, hedge, model-risk and survival systems independent of alpha** — Capital, hedge, model-risk and survival systems are independent from alpha generation: no alpha/model component can construct, relax or bypass their decisions. |  | acceptance: Survival/risk crates do not depend on alpha/model crates and alpha crates cannot construct a permit. | v11.6 p50 §31.1 v12 Superintelligence Completeness Additions |

## ASSET

Asset / Portfolio Brain.

| ID | P | Kind | Requirement | Flags | Verification | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R12-ASSET-1015 | P2 | capability | **Class-specific adapters and lifecycle engines per asset class** — Liquid, event-driven, illiquid, private, tokenized and physical assets are supported through class-specific adapters and lifecycle engines. |  | integration: An asset-class registry maps each of the six classes to an adapter and lifecycle engine; an unregistered class is refused. | v11.6 p3 §1.1 Product Identity |
| R12-ASSET-1160 | P2 | capability | **Asset management capability scope** — The platform provides Asset management with the required scope: Portfolio construction, mandates, horizons, valuation, lifecycle, corporate actions, commitments, divestment, hedging and attribution. |  | inspection: Each scope item of 'Asset management' maps to code reached from a composition root with a named test; a missing item fails inspection. | v11.6 p6 §3 Full Capability Map |
| R12-ASSET-3017 | P2 | capability | **Crypto/On-chain Brain** — A Crypto/On-chain Brain exists as a specialist covering: protocol state, token economics, bridges, liquidity, smart contracts and on-chain flows. |  | integration: The Crypto/On-chain Brain is constructed in the production organisation and emits findings for its scope; removing it drops its scope from coverage. | v11.6 p16 §9.1 Specialist Brain Society |
| R12-ASSET-3018 | P2 | capability | **Private Markets Brain** — A Private Markets Brain exists as a specialist covering: illiquid valuations, commitments, capital calls, private-company/fund lifecycle. |  | integration: The Private Markets Brain is constructed in the production organisation and emits findings for its scope; removing it drops its scope from coverage. | v11.6 p16 §9.1 Specialist Brain Society |
| R12-ASSET-3019 | P2 | capability | **Portfolio/Asset Brain** — A Portfolio/Asset Brain exists as a specialist covering: mandates, horizons, exposures, hedges, divestment, factor/causal-driver allocation. |  | integration: The Portfolio/Asset Brain is constructed in the production organisation and emits findings for its scope; removing it drops its scope from coverage. | v11.6 p16 §9.1 Specialist Brain Society |
| R12-ASSET-4065 | P1 | constraint | **Asset Brain owns economic lifecycle, not execution mechanics** — The Asset Brain owns the economic lifecycle of positions and does not perform execution mechanics. |  | acceptance: Position economics types (qip-portfolio, qip-financial position records) have no dependency on execution/broker crates. | v11.6 p26 §12 Asset Management Brain |
| R12-ASSET-4066 | P1 | invariant | **Per-position mandate and target exposure** — For every position the Asset Brain maintains the governing mandate and target exposure. |  | integration: Every held position resolves to a mandate and a target exposure; a position without one is refused or flagged. | v11.6 p26 §12 Asset Management Brain |
| R12-ASSET-4067 | P1 | invariant | **Valuation confidence, horizon and expected liquidity per position** — For every position the Asset Brain maintains valuation confidence, investment horizon and expected liquidity. |  | integration: A position's record carries all three; stale valuation confidence refuses sizing. | v11.6 p26 §12 Asset Management Brain |
| R12-ASSET-4068 | P1 | invariant | **Hedges, corporate actions and commitments per position** — For every position the Asset Brain maintains its hedges, corporate actions and commitments. |  | integration: Each position resolves its hedge relationship, pending corporate actions and commitments, with 'none' stated rather than inferred. | v11.6 p26 §12 Asset Management Brain |
| R12-ASSET-4069 | P1 | invariant | **Exit plan and attribution per position** — For every position the Asset Brain maintains an exit plan and attribution. |  | integration: A position cannot be opened without an exit plan; its P&L attributes exactly to sources. | v11.6 p26 §12 Asset Management Brain |
| R12-ASSET-4070 | P1 | capability | **Portfolio construction across strategies, classes, regions, horizons** — Portfolio construction spans strategies, asset classes, regions and horizons jointly. |  | integration: One construction run respects per-strategy, per-class, per-region and per-horizon budgets together. | v11.6 p26 §12 Asset Management Brain |
| R12-ASSET-4071 | P1 | invariant | **Position lifecycle discover through attribute** — Each position follows discover -> evaluate -> acquire -> hold/hedge -> monitor -> resize -> exit/divest -> settle -> attribute, and nothing reaches attribute without settling. |  | unit: Illegal transitions are refused; exit cannot reach attribute without settle. | v11.6 p26 §12 Asset Management Brain |
| R12-ASSET-4072 | P2 | capability | **Illiquid/private mode** — An illiquid/private mode handles commitments, capital calls, marks, lockups and secondary-exit logic. |  | integration: A private position records commitments and calls, carries a dated mark, refuses exit inside a lockup and plans a secondary exit at an evidenced discount. | v11.6 p26 §12 Asset Management Brain |
| R12-ASSET-4073 | P3 | capability | **Physical mode with inventory, logistics and product economics** — A physical mode integrates inventory/logistics and product-level economics into the asset lifecycle. | `EXTERNAL_ACTION` | integration: A physical position carries landed cost and inventory state from the inventory ledger. | v11.6 p26 §12 Asset Management Brain |
| R12-ASSET-4074 | P0 | invariant | **Continuous reconciliation of economic exposure and legal ownership** — Economic exposure is continuously reconciled against legal/settled ownership, and a break stops the affected scope. |  | integration: LEARN reconciles the ledger view against custody statements each cycle; a break halts the scope; a test injects a mismatch. | v11.6 p26 §12 Asset Management Brain |

## LEDGER

Ledger, accounting, settlement, reconciliation.

| ID | P | Kind | Requirement | Flags | Verification | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R12-LEDGER-8007 | P0 | constraint | **Financial truth in Spanner Enterprise Plus** — Ledger, positions, obligations, capital grants and idempotent outcome processing must live in a strongly consistent Spanner Enterprise Plus database as the financial system of record. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | integration: A ledger write commits through a Spanner adapter in one transaction; pointing the writer at a non-Spanner store in production config must be refused. | GCP v2.1 p2 §1 Executive Architecture; GCP v2.1 p10 §10 Ledger, Capital and Financial State |
| R12-LEDGER-8024 | P0 | invariant | **Financial writers enforce uniqueness transactionally** — Financial writers must enforce event uniqueness inside the same transaction that applies the effect. |  | property: For any duplication/redelivery/crash schedule, balances equal one clean delivery; splitting the write batch must fail. | GCP v2.1 p3 §2 Non-Negotiable Architecture Rules |
| R12-LEDGER-8132 | P0 | constraint | **Spanner Enterprise Plus multi-region with external consistency** — The authoritative ledger/accounting database must be Spanner Enterprise Plus in a multi-region configuration with external consistency. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` `MULTI_REGION` `COST` | plan: Plan shows edition ENTERPRISE_PLUS and a multi-region config. | GCP v2.1 p10 §10 Ledger, Capital and Financial State |
| R12-LEDGER-8133 | P1 | constraint | **Separate instances for ledger and world graph** — Ledger and world-graph databases must be on separate Spanner instances/databases to avoid noisy-neighbour coupling. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` `COST` | plan: Plan shows distinct instances for ledger and graph. | GCP v2.1 p10 §10 Ledger, Capital and Financial State |
| R12-LEDGER-8134 | P0 | contract | **Ledger writer consumes P1 transactionally keyed by event_id** — A regional ledger-writer must consume P1 outcome partitions and, in one transaction keyed by event_id, update double-entry postings/positions/cash and record the processed event ID so duplicate delivery is a no-op. |  | property: Any redelivery/crash schedule yields balances equal to one clean delivery; process reaches apply from main. | GCP v2.1 p10 §10 Ledger, Capital and Financial State |
| R12-LEDGER-8135 | P0 | invariant | **Ledger is asynchronous to order placement** — The ledger must be asynchronous to order placement so global consistency never slows the hot path. |  | acceptance: No edge crate reaches the storage/ledger crates; the cell places without a ledger call. | GCP v2.1 p10 §10 Ledger, Capital and Financial State |
| R12-LEDGER-8136 | P2 | process | **Spanner leader region chosen by write origin and residency** — The Spanner configuration/leader region must be chosen by write origin and legal residency. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` `MULTI_REGION` | plan: Plan sets default_leader with recorded rationale. | GCP v2.1 p10 §10 Ledger, Capital and Financial State |
| R12-LEDGER-8137 | P1 | constraint | **Spanner managed autoscaling with high minimum; hotspot-safe keys** — Spanner must use managed autoscaling with a deliberately high minimum sized for peak close/reconciliation, and keys must be hotspot-safe. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` `COST` | plan: Plan shows autoscaling_config with min ≥ sized floor; schema keys not monotonic. | GCP v2.1 p11 §10 Ledger, Capital and Financial State |
| R12-LEDGER-8138 | P2 | constraint | **Change streams feed analytics only, never the system of record** — Spanner change streams/Dataflow may feed BigQuery/reporting and derived caches, which must remain downstream analytics and never the system of record. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | inspection: No component reads balances from a derived copy for a decision. | GCP v2.1 p11 §10 Ledger, Capital and Financial State |
| R12-LEDGER-8139 | P0 | process | **Continuous venue/custody vs ledger reconciliation halts deterministically** — Venue/custody truth must be continuously compared with the ledger; discrepancies must raise a P0/P1 Fabric event and can narrow or stop local execution under deterministic policy. |  | integration: An injected break emits a fabric event and halts/narrows the cell; removing the trip must fail. | GCP v2.1 p11 §10 Ledger, Capital and Financial State |
| R12-LEDGER-8140 | P0 | capability | **Scheduled Spanner backups and PITR** — The ledger must have scheduled Spanner backups and point-in-time recovery. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | plan: Plan shows a backup schedule and version_retention_period. | GCP v2.1 p11 §10 Ledger, Capital and Financial State |
| R12-LEDGER-8141 | P0 | capability | **Immutable ledger exports and audit manifests to locked GCS** — Immutable ledger exports and audit manifests must be written to locked GCS for independent reconstruction. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | integration: An export lands in a retention-locked bucket and replays to the same balances. | GCP v2.1 p11 §10 Ledger, Capital and Financial State |
| R12-LEDGER-10041 | P0 | constraint | **Spanner Enterprise Plus is authoritative financial truth** — Ledger, positions, obligations, capital and idempotency records live in Spanner Enterprise Plus with multi-region HA, isolated from analytics workloads. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` `MULTI_REGION` `NEW_DEPENDENCY` | integration: A ledger writer commits to a multi-region Enterprise Plus database and no analytics workload reads it directly. | GCP v2.1 p21 §19 Complete GCP Service Catalog for This Platform; GCP v2.1 p22 §19.0B Data, Knowledge & Financial Truth; GCP v2.1 p29 §29 Current GCP Capability Validation Notes (2026) |
| R12-LEDGER-10082 | P0 | invariant | **Ledger writer commits outcomes idempotently** — The ledger writer consumes P1 outcomes and commits them to the authoritative store idempotently, so redelivery and crash never double-book or drop. |  | property: For any duplication/redelivery/crash schedule, balances equal a single clean delivery; mutating the dedup rule fails. | GCP v2.1 p24 §20 Critical Service-to-Service Connection Matrix; GCP v2.1 p25 §21.1 Tick to order and learning; GCP v2.1 p28 §27 Architecture Acceptance Criteria |
| R12-LEDGER-10114 | P0 | contract | **All arbitrage intents, fills, hedges and outcomes are journaled and ledgered** — All intents, fills, hedges and outcomes of multi-region cycles are journaled to Fabric and ledgered centrally; replay evaluates missed/failed cycles. |  | integration: A cycle's events appear in P1 and the ledger balances reflect them. | GCP v2.1 p25 §21.3 Multi-region arbitrage |
| R12-LEDGER-10163 | P0 | process | **Build phase 4: Financial truth** — Phase 4 (Financial truth) delivers: Spanner ledger writer/idempotency, reconciliation, capital/risk envelope contracts, meeting its exit test before later phases depend on it. |  | inspection: Each listed deliverable exists and its exit test passes. | GCP v2.1 p28 §26 Recommended Build Order |
| R12-LEDGER-10179 | P0 | invariant | **Every financial outcome commits exactly once in effect via event-id idempotency** — Every financial outcome is eventually committed exactly once in effect to the authoritative ledger through event-id idempotency, even when delivered multiple times. |  | property: Property test: any redelivery/crash schedule yields single-delivery balances. | GCP v2.1 p28 §27 Architecture Acceptance Criteria |
| R12-LEDGER-10185 | P0 | invariant | **Reconciliation rebuilds positions and cash from ledger plus venue/custody statements** — Reconciliation rebuilds and verifies positions and cash from the authoritative ledger plus venue/custody statements without trusting caches. |  | integration: Refolding the outcome stream equals stored balances; venue drop-copy reconciles; breaks are counted by direction. | GCP v2.1 p29 §27 Architecture Acceptance Criteria |
| R12-LEDGER-10197 | P0 | invariant | **Must not share one Spanner database across trust domains** — Ledger, world graph, agent memory and service tables must not share one Spanner database; trust/performance domains are separated. |  | inspection: Each Spanner database serves one domain; a second domain's tables in the ledger DB fail. | GCP v2.1 p29 §28 Explicit Anti-Patterns; GCP v2.1 p29 §29 Current GCP Capability Validation Notes (2026) |
| R12-LEDGER-1164 | P1 | capability | **Ledger/accounting capability scope** — The platform provides Ledger/accounting with the required scope: Event-sourced double-entry multi-asset ledger: positions, cash, obligations, inventory, tax lots, fees, accruals, margin, commitments and settlement state. |  | inspection: Each scope item of 'Ledger/accounting' maps to code reached from a composition root with a named test; a missing item fails inspection. | v11.6 p7 §3 Full Capability Map |
| R12-LEDGER-1197 | P0 | contract | **Fills/events flow to ledger, treasury, settlement and inventory** — Fills and events from cells flow to ledger, treasury, settlement and inventory. |  | integration: A cell fill reported to the centre produces balanced ledger postings and inventory update. | v11.6 p12 §5 End-to-End System Path |
| R12-LEDGER-5001 | P0 | invariant | **Ledger is append-only and event-sourced** — The ledger accepts only appends of immutable events; no posting or balance can be updated or deleted, and balances are a fold of the event stream. |  | integration: Re-folding the stored event stream from its first record equals stored balances; mutation: add an update path or skip an event in the fold and the refold test fails. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
| R12-LEDGER-5002 | P0 | invariant | **Every ledger event balances per unit** — Each ledger event is double-entry: at least two positive postings whose debits equal credits exactly in every unit, refused otherwise (never plugged). |  | property: Constructing or deserialising an unbalanced event is refused; mutation: remove the per-unit sum check and the refusal test fails. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
| R12-LEDGER-5003 | P1 | capability | **Ledger is multi-asset** — The ledger holds balances per account per unit for any asset (currencies, instruments, physical units), not a single currency. |  | unit: Postings in two units for one event are balanced independently and both balances are readable; mutation: key balances by account only and the test fails. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
| R12-LEDGER-5004 | P0 | invariant | **Ledger authoritative for cash and positions** — Cash and instrument positions are booked in the ledger from fills and the ledger, not a model or cache, is the authority for them. |  | integration: The state is readable from ledger events after the producing event is applied; mutation: drop the posting and the balance check fails. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
| R12-LEDGER-5005 | P1 | capability | **Ledger authoritative for inventory** — Inventory (trading inventory and reservations against it) is held as ledger state. |  | integration: The state is readable from ledger events after the producing event is applied; mutation: drop the posting and the balance check fails. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
| R12-LEDGER-5006 | P1 | capability | **Ledger authoritative for obligations and commitments** — Obligations and commitments (e.g. unfunded private commitments, pending payables) are recorded as ledger state. |  | integration: The state is readable from ledger events after the producing event is applied; mutation: drop the posting and the balance check fails. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
| R12-LEDGER-5007 | P0 | invariant | **Ledger records venue-reported fees** — Fees are booked in the ledger to a fees account equal to the venue-reported fee, and no fee is booked when the venue did not report one. |  | integration: The state is readable from ledger events after the producing event is applied; mutation: drop the posting and the balance check fails. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
| R12-LEDGER-5008 | P1 | capability | **Ledger tracks tax lots** — Tax lots are ledger state, so cost basis and holding period per lot are derivable from the ledger. |  | integration: The state is readable from ledger events after the producing event is applied; mutation: drop the posting and the balance check fails. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
| R12-LEDGER-5009 | P0 | invariant | **Ledger tracks collateral and margin** — Collateral pledged and margin requirements are recorded as ledger state. |  | integration: The state is readable from ledger events after the producing event is applied; mutation: drop the posting and the balance check fails. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
| R12-LEDGER-5010 | P1 | capability | **Ledger records transfers** — Transfers of cash or assets between accounts, venues or regions are ledger events. |  | integration: The state is readable from ledger events after the producing event is applied; mutation: drop the posting and the balance check fails. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
| R12-LEDGER-5011 | P0 | invariant | **Ledger records settlement state** — Each ledger event carries its settlement state through to settlement. |  | integration: The state is readable from ledger events after the producing event is applied; mutation: drop the posting and the balance check fails. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
| R12-LEDGER-5012 | P0 | invariant | **Every posting cites the observed fact that caused it** — Ledger state is never inferred: every event names the cell, session, journal sequence/digest and order that produced it, and an event without a source is refused. |  | unit: An event with an empty source field is refused; mutation: remove refuse_unnamed for the digest and the test fails. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
| R12-LEDGER-5013 | P0 | invariant | **Global journal with idempotent event IDs** — One global journal applies each event exactly once: redelivery of the same event ID changes nothing, and the same ID with different content is an integrity break. |  | property: Any duplication/redelivery/crash sequence yields balances equal to one clean delivery; mutation: apply duplicates and the property fails. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
| R12-LEDGER-5014 | P0 | invariant | **Immutable lineage from intent to cash impact** — Each ledger event carries an immutable lineage intent -> order -> fill -> settlement -> position/cash impact that can be walked in both directions. |  | integration: Given a balance change, a reader recovers the order, journal entry and originating intent; mutation: drop order_id from SourceEvent and the walk fails. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
| R12-LEDGER-5015 | P1 | capability | **Ledger has a trading subledger** — The ledger maintains a trading subledger whose postings roll up into the global double-entry journal. |  | unit: A trading event posts to the trading subledger and the global trial balance still balances; mutation: route it elsewhere and the test fails. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
| R12-LEDGER-5016 | P1 | capability | **Ledger has a treasury subledger** — The ledger maintains a treasury subledger whose postings roll up into the global double-entry journal. |  | unit: A treasury event posts to the treasury subledger and the global trial balance still balances; mutation: route it elsewhere and the test fails. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
| R12-LEDGER-5017 | P1 | capability | **Ledger has a custody subledger** — The ledger maintains a custody subledger whose postings roll up into the global double-entry journal. |  | unit: A custody event posts to the custody subledger and the global trial balance still balances; mutation: route it elsewhere and the test fails. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
| R12-LEDGER-5018 | P2 | capability | **Ledger has a physical inventory/commerce subledger** — The ledger maintains a physical inventory/commerce subledger whose postings roll up into the global double-entry journal. |  | unit: A physical inventory/commerce event posts to the physical inventory/commerce subledger and the global trial balance still balances; mutation: route it elsewhere and the test fails. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
| R12-LEDGER-5019 | P1 | capability | **Ledger has a fees/taxes subledger** — The ledger maintains a fees/taxes subledger whose postings roll up into the global double-entry journal. |  | unit: A fees/taxes event posts to the fees/taxes subledger and the global trial balance still balances; mutation: route it elsewhere and the test fails. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
| R12-LEDGER-5020 | P2 | capability | **Ledger has a private commitments subledger** — The ledger maintains a private commitments subledger whose postings roll up into the global double-entry journal. |  | unit: A private commitments event posts to the private commitments subledger and the global trial balance still balances; mutation: route it elsewhere and the test fails. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
| R12-LEDGER-5021 | P1 | capability | **Ledger has a user/mandate ownership subledger** — The ledger maintains a user/mandate ownership subledger whose postings roll up into the global double-entry journal. |  | unit: A user/mandate ownership event posts to the user/mandate ownership subledger and the global trial balance still balances; mutation: route it elsewhere and the test fails. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
| R12-LEDGER-5022 | P0 | process | **Continuous external reconciliation with venues** — The ledger is continuously reconciled against venues' own records and every break is recorded and acted on. |  | integration: A seeded difference between ledger and venues record produces a recorded break; mutation: disable the comparison and no break is recorded. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
| R12-LEDGER-5023 | P1 | process | **Continuous external reconciliation with banks** — The ledger is continuously reconciled against banks' own records and every break is recorded and acted on. | `EXTERNAL_ACTION` | integration: A seeded difference between ledger and banks record produces a recorded break; mutation: disable the comparison and no break is recorded. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
| R12-LEDGER-5024 | P1 | process | **Continuous external reconciliation with custodians** — The ledger is continuously reconciled against custodians' own records and every break is recorded and acted on. | `EXTERNAL_ACTION` | integration: A seeded difference between ledger and custodians record produces a recorded break; mutation: disable the comparison and no break is recorded. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
| R12-LEDGER-5025 | P2 | process | **Continuous external reconciliation with wallets** — The ledger is continuously reconciled against wallets' own records and every break is recorded and acted on. |  | integration: A seeded difference between ledger and wallets record produces a recorded break; mutation: disable the comparison and no break is recorded. | v11.6 p28 §15 Ledger, Accounting & State of Truth |
