## DATA

Scout fabric, source manifests, pass-through data, knowledge tiers, storage.

| ID | P | Kind | Requirement | Flags | Verification | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R12-DATA-8006 | P2 | capability | **Data/Intelligence plane on GCS, Bigtable, BigQuery, Catalog, Spanner Graph** — Pass-through world sensing, retained tick/internal history, compact provenance/knowledge, research and models must be served by GCS, Bigtable, BigQuery, Knowledge Catalog, Spanner Graph and Agent Platform/Vertex APIs. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | plan: Plan provisions each store and an in-tree adapter opens it; an adapter refusing the target must fail the integration test. | GCP v2.1 p2 §1 Executive Architecture |
| R12-DATA-8011 | P0 | invariant | **External world payloads are transient and discarded after distillation** — External world content must be parsed, evidence-scored and distilled into durable knowledge, after which the source payload is discarded; GCP storage holds Algorik-created knowledge, not a mirror of the public world. |  | unit: The durable record of an ingested document has no field able to hold source text; adding a body String to NewsItem must fail a structural test. | GCP v2.1 p2 §2.1 World Data Pass-Through / Knowledge Persistence Invariant |
| R12-DATA-8012 | P1 | constraint | **No default GCS landing bucket for external-world content** — Crawler/scout workers must stream source bytes through memory or encrypted ephemeral disk only; there must be no default GCS landing bucket for external-world content. |  | inspection: No Terraform bucket is designated for external content; adding one must fail an acceptance scan. | GCP v2.1 p2 §2.1 World Data Pass-Through / Knowledge Persistence Invariant |
| R12-DATA-8013 | P1 | contract | **Source manifest records the full re-fetch descriptor** — Each ingested source must have a manifest recording canonical URI/provider ID, retrieval time, version/etag, content hash, entitlement, cursor, freshness, parser lineage and re-fetch instructions. |  | unit: Constructing a manifest without any of the nine fields is refused; dropping a field from the type must fail a round-trip test. | GCP v2.1 p2 §2.1 World Data Pass-Through / Knowledge Persistence Invariant |
| R12-DATA-8014 | P1 | capability | **Source Manifest Registry persisted in Spanner/AlloyDB** — Source manifests must be held in a queryable Source Manifest Registry backed by Spanner or AlloyDB. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | integration: A registry write and lookup by canonical URI round-trips through the Spanner/AlloyDB adapter. | GCP v2.1 p2 §2.1 World Data Pass-Through / Knowledge Persistence Invariant |
| R12-DATA-8015 | P1 | constraint | **Knowledge graph holds entities, edges, beliefs — never copied documents** — The knowledge graph (Spanner Graph, tier K1) must store entities, relationships, temporal facts, causal edges, source references, beliefs, confidence and contradictions, and never copied documents. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | integration: Graph node/edge schema has no document-body field and a write of a copied document is refused. | GCP v2.1 p3 §2.1 World Data Pass-Through / Knowledge Persistence Invariant; GCP v2.1 p10 §9.2 Durable Knowledge Tiers |
| R12-DATA-8016 | P2 | capability | **Bigtable holds fast derived knowledge state** — High-rate derived features, entity/event state, ambient-model state and time-indexed knowledge deltas must be stored in Bigtable. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | integration: Feature/state deltas written via a Bigtable adapter are read back by time index. | GCP v2.1 p3 §2.1 World Data Pass-Through / Knowledge Persistence Invariant |
| R12-DATA-8017 | P2 | capability | **Knowledge packs in GCS with lifecycle tiers** — Compact Algorik-derived knowledge packs (protobuf/Parquet snapshots, embeddings, summaries, world-model deltas, model artifacts) must be stored in GCS under Standard/Nearline/Coldline lifecycle tiers (tier K2). | `MANAGED_SERVICE` `NEW_DEPENDENCY` | plan: Plan shows a pack bucket with lifecycle transitions; a pack writer stores only derived artifacts. | GCP v2.1 p3 §2.1 World Data Pass-Through / Knowledge Persistence Invariant; GCP v2.1 p10 §9.2 Durable Knowledge Tiers |
| R12-DATA-8018 | P2 | process | **Historical world analysis re-fetches via manifest, never copies** — Historical world analysis must re-fetch from the authoritative source/provider using the manifest and process transiently; licensed historical APIs are queried on demand instead of copying corpora. | `EXTERNAL_ACTION` | integration: A replay of a past document triggers a manifest-driven re-fetch whose hash is compared with the manifest; a cached copy path must not exist. | GCP v2.1 p3 §2.1 World Data Pass-Through / Knowledge Persistence Invariant |
| R12-DATA-8019 | P1 | constraint | **Tick and internal financial data are separate retained classes (Tier T)** — Tick/order-book data and Algorik-owned financial/execution data must be distinct retention classes, archived (GCS/Tick Lake) under entitlements when rights/policy require replay, accounting, audit or training. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | unit: Each topic maps to exactly one retention class; tick and financial classes differ and archive with entitlement labels. | GCP v2.1 p3 §2.1 World Data Pass-Through / Knowledge Persistence Invariant; GCP v2.1 p10 §9.2 Durable Knowledge Tiers |
| R12-DATA-8112 | P1 | constraint | **Crawler/scout workers isolated from trading** — Crawler/scout workers must run in isolated pools or Cloud Run jobs with egress, quotas and security posture separate from trading. | `KUBERNETES` | plan: Scouts run under their own identity and egress path; that identity holds no trading permission. | GCP v2.1 p9 §8 Regional GKE Service Plane |
| R12-DATA-8120 | P1 | process | **World sensing pipeline: egress → scout → parse → evidence → delta** — World sensing must flow external source → secure egress → transient scout worker → parse/extract → evidence/provenance → knowledge delta, recording manifest/hash/cursor and writing to graph/Bigtable/memory, with the raw payload discarded. |  | integration: A fixture source traverses every stage; the output delta carries manifest and provenance and no payload. | GCP v2.1 p9 §9.1 Pass-Through World Sensing Pipeline |
| R12-DATA-8121 | P1 | constraint | **No general-purpose raw-world-data lake** — There must be no general-purpose raw-world-data lake; the durable plane stores only Algorik's compact interpretation plus re-visit metadata. |  | inspection: No store holds raw external payloads; a lake-shaped resource fails review. | GCP v2.1 p10 §9.1 Pass-Through World Sensing Pipeline |
| R12-DATA-8122 | P2 | capability | **Tier K0 live ambient state** — Tier K0 must hold seconds-to-hours of compact derived state in memory/caches and Bigtable. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | integration: Derived state is retained in memory with a bounded horizon and spilled to Bigtable. | GCP v2.1 p10 §9.2 Durable Knowledge Tiers |
| R12-DATA-8123 | P1 | constraint | **Tier K3 BigQuery holds derived facts, not copied documents** — Tier K3 BigQuery tables must contain derived facts/features/outcomes and research datasets, never copied external documents. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | integration: Warehouse rows schema has no document-body column. | GCP v2.1 p10 §9.2 Durable Knowledge Tiers |
| R12-DATA-8124 | P1 | capability | **GCS regional landing for tick/internal segments via private APIs** — A regional GCS landing store must hold retained tick/internal replay segments and knowledge packs (never raw external corpus), written by fabric-archive over private Google APIs. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | plan: Plan shows a regional landing bucket; fabricd writes to it via PGA. | GCP v2.1 p10 §9.2 Durable Knowledge Tiers |
| R12-DATA-8125 | P0 | constraint | **Durable research/archive with Bucket Lock and versioning** — The durable research/archive bucket must receive data by lifecycle from landing and use Bucket Lock/versioning where retention must be immutable. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | plan: Plan shows locked retention and versioning on the archive bucket. | GCP v2.1 p10 §9.2 Durable Knowledge Tiers |
| R12-DATA-8126 | P2 | capability | **Bigtable multi-cluster with routing app profiles** — Bigtable must be multi-cluster with multi-cluster routing for read resilience and app profiles chosen by consistency need, fed by fabric-sink-bigtable. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` `COST` | plan: Plan shows ≥2 clusters and app profiles. | GCP v2.1 p10 §9.2 Durable Knowledge Tiers |
| R12-DATA-8127 | P1 | constraint | **BigQuery fed by sinks/batch loads, isolated from hot path** — BigQuery must be fed via fabric-sink-bigquery or batch loads from GCS and stay isolated from the hot path. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | acceptance: No edge crate reaches the warehouse; loads come from the fabric/GCS. | GCP v2.1 p10 §9.2 Durable Knowledge Tiers |
| R12-DATA-8128 | P2 | capability | **Knowledge Catalog registers lineage and evidence contracts** — Knowledge Catalog must register GCS/BigQuery/Spanner/AI lineage, quality and evidence contracts to ground agents in governed context. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | plan: Catalog entries exist for each data product with lineage. | GCP v2.1 p10 §9.2 Durable Knowledge Tiers |
| R12-DATA-8130 | P2 | capability | **AlloyDB for non-financial operational metadata** — AlloyDB must hold operational relational metadata/workflow state (admin/research workflow, agent jobs, config history) that is not financial truth. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | integration: Workflow state persists via an AlloyDB adapter; ledger tables are absent from it. | GCP v2.1 p10 §9.2 Durable Knowledge Tiers |
| R12-DATA-8131 | P3 | constraint | **Dataflow/Dataproc for batch reprocessing only** — Dataflow/Dataproc Serverless must be used for large batch reprocessing from durable GCS/BigQuery sources and must not be the primary live fabric consumer path. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` `NON_RUST` | inspection: Batch jobs read GCS/BQ only; no live fabric subscription. | GCP v2.1 p10 §9.2 Durable Knowledge Tiers |
| R12-DATA-9052 | P1 | constraint | **Spanner Graph holds promoted durable entities, never agent scratchpads** — Spanner Graph holds promoted durable entities, relationships, causal edges, world-model state and provenance references, and never agent scratchpads. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | unit: Only promoted entities can be written to the graph store; a scratchpad write is refused. | GCP v2.1 p14 §11.8 Superintelligence Memory Boundaries |
| R12-DATA-9053 | P2 | constraint | **Bigtable holds high-rate ambient state, reputation, scores, features, branch indexes** — Bigtable holds high-rate ambient state, forecast reputation, model scores, live derived features and branch indexes. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | unit: These record kinds are routed to the wide-column target and nothing else is. | GCP v2.1 p14 §11.8 Superintelligence Memory Boundaries |
| R12-DATA-9054 | P2 | constraint | **BigQuery holds resolved outcomes, tournament history, research analytics** — BigQuery holds resolved forecast outcomes, tournament history, research analytics and large derived datasets. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | integration: Resolved outcomes are streamed to the warehouse. | GCP v2.1 p14 §11.8 Superintelligence Memory Boundaries |
| R12-DATA-9055 | P1 | constraint | **Cloud Storage holds retained history, segments, artifacts; never raw-world corpus** — Cloud Storage holds retained tick/internal history, immutable Fabric segments, model artifacts and compact knowledge/world-state packs, and is never a general raw-world corpus. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | integration: Archiver writes sealed segments and artifacts with deterministic names; writes outside the named classes are refused. | GCP v2.1 p14 §11.8 Superintelligence Memory Boundaries |
| R12-DATA-9184 | P2 | constraint | **Bigtable autoscaling with hotspot-safe row keys and per-workload app profiles** — Bigtable uses managed autoscaling, hotspot-safe row keys and workload-specific app profiles. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | plan: Autoscaling config, row-key design tests and app profiles exist. | GCP v2.1 p19 §18 Scaling and Performance Model |
| R12-DATA-9185 | P2 | constraint | **Spanner autoscaling with high floor; schema eliminates hotspots** — Spanner uses managed autoscaling with a high floor, and schema/key design eliminates hotspots before scaling. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` `COST` | plan: Autoscaling config with min ≥ floor. | GCP v2.1 p19 §18 Scaling and Performance Model |
| R12-DATA-9187 | P1 | constraint | **GCS partitioned, immutable, reasonably sized segments; tiny objects compacted async** — GCS objects are partitioned by source/date/instrument, written as immutable reasonably sized segments, and tiny objects are compacted asynchronously. |  | unit: Archive names partition by source/date and compaction merges small objects off the write path. | GCP v2.1 p19 §18 Scaling and Performance Model |
| R12-DATA-10033 | P1 | capability | **Cloud Storage holds replay lake, segment archive, models and evidence** — Cloud Storage holds the tick/internal replay lake, Fabric segment archive, model weights, compact knowledge packs and internal evidence, under lifecycle classes. |  | plan: Buckets for archive, artifacts and evidence exist with lifecycle/retention rules; fabricd archives to a GCS blob store. | GCP v2.1 p20 §19 Complete GCP Service Catalog for This Platform; GCP v2.1 p22 §19.0B Data, Knowledge & Financial Truth |
| R12-DATA-10034 | P0 | invariant | **External world payloads are never retained** — Raw external-world content is not warehoused: only derived knowledge packs and internal/tick history are retained. |  | unit: A knowledge pack carrying an undeclared field (e.g. a raw body) is refused at decode; removing deny_unknown_fields fails the test. | GCP v2.1 p20 §19 Complete GCP Service Catalog for This Platform; GCP v2.1 p22 §19.0B Data, Knowledge & Financial Truth; GCP v2.1 p30 §31 Final Platform Definition |
| R12-DATA-10035 | P2 | capability | **Bigtable holds rebuildable high-rate derived state** — Bigtable stores high-rate derived time-series, features and replay indexes, rebuildable from retained sources, with replication only as justified. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | plan: Instance is planned with autoscaling; a rebuild job from GCS/Fabric repopulates it. | GCP v2.1 p20 §19 Complete GCP Service Catalog for This Platform; GCP v2.1 p22 §19.0B Data, Knowledge & Financial Truth |
| R12-DATA-10036 | P2 | capability | **BigQuery serves research and may lag without trading impact** — BigQuery holds research, resolved forecast scores, backtests and analytics; it can lag without trading impact. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | integration: The research dataset exists and the edge node links no BigQuery client. | GCP v2.1 p20 §19 Complete GCP Service Catalog for This Platform; GCP v2.1 p22 §19.0B Data, Knowledge & Financial Truth |
| R12-DATA-10037 | P2 | capability | **Knowledge Catalog provides governance, lineage and quality** — Knowledge Catalog (Dataplex) records governance, lineage, quality and data products for platform data. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | plan: Catalog entries/lineage exist for the data stores. | GCP v2.1 p20 §19 Complete GCP Service Catalog for This Platform; GCP v2.1 p30 §29 Current GCP Capability Validation Notes (2026) |
| R12-DATA-10038 | P2 | invariant | **Dataflow is batch/reprocessing only, never the live bus** — Dataflow (and Dataproc Serverless) is used only for large batch/reprocessing and Spanner change-stream pipelines, never as the primary live bus. |  | inspection: No Dataflow job/resource consumes or produces a live Fabric stream. | GCP v2.1 p20 §19 Complete GCP Service Catalog for This Platform |
| R12-DATA-10039 | P0 | invariant | **Memorystore is a non-authoritative cache only** — Memorystore/Redis holds only non-authoritative warm cache; its loss causes recompute or cache miss, never financial inconsistency. |  | integration: Every composition root refuses Memorystore as a store of record; persistence is disabled; setting a root's store to Redis stops start-up. | GCP v2.1 p20 §19 Complete GCP Service Catalog for This Platform; GCP v2.1 p22 §19.0B Data, Knowledge & Financial Truth |
| R12-DATA-10040 | P2 | capability | **AlloyDB holds operational relational metadata** — Operational relational metadata and workflows live in AlloyDB. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | plan: AlloyDB cluster/instance planned with a writer. | GCP v2.1 p20 §19 Complete GCP Service Catalog for This Platform |
| R12-DATA-10079 | P1 | capability | **Fabric segments archive immutably to Cloud Storage** — Sealed Fabric segments are archived immutably to Cloud Storage via private Google access, off the commit path, as a replay source. |  | integration: fabricd archives sealed segments to a GCS store; a stalled store does not block produce. | GCP v2.1 p23 §20 Critical Service-to-Service Connection Matrix; GCP v2.1 p25 §21.1 Tick to order and learning |
| R12-DATA-10080 | P2 | capability | **Fabric sink writes recent features and replay indexes to Bigtable** — A Fabric sink writes recent features, time series and replay indexes to Bigtable asynchronously. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` `NEW_DEPENDENCY` | integration: Sink consumes a stream and rows appear in Bigtable. | GCP v2.1 p23 §20 Critical Service-to-Service Connection Matrix; GCP v2.1 p25 §21.1 Tick to order and learning |
| R12-DATA-10081 | P2 | capability | **Fabric BigQuery sink streams research data and may lag** — A Fabric sink streams research/analytics records to BigQuery (Storage Write API) and may lag without trading impact. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | integration: Sink consumes a stream and rows land; sink lag does not affect producers. | GCP v2.1 p23 §20 Critical Service-to-Service Connection Matrix |
| R12-DATA-10164 | P1 | process | **Build phase 5: Data plane** — Phase 5 (Data plane) delivers: GCS retained tick/internal archive and compact knowledge packs, Bigtable, BigQuery, Knowledge Catalog, replay/feature sinks, data contracts, meeting its exit test before later phases depend on it. |  | inspection: Each listed deliverable exists and its exit test passes. | GCP v2.1 p28 §26 Recommended Build Order |
| R12-DATA-10206 | P2 | constraint | **Bigtable app profiles match consistency needs** — Bigtable routing/app-profile choice matches each reader/writer's consistency needs. |  | plan: An app profile per access pattern with matching routing. | GCP v2.1 p29 §29 Current GCP Capability Validation Notes (2026) |
| R12-DATA-1029 | P0 | constraint | **Do not warehouse raw external world content** — External world content (web pages, filings, news, social, product pages, regulatory records, geospatial feeds) is processed transiently and discarded after evidence and knowledge are extracted. |  | unit: The durable stores have no class that can hold a world-source document; an attempt is refused by name. | v11.6 p3 §1.3 External World Data Is Pass-Through; Knowledge Is Durable |
| R12-DATA-1030 | P0 | contract | **Source manifest persists source reference and provider identity** — Each processed external source persists a source reference (locator) and provider/source identity. |  | unit: A reference built from a real fetch digest carries a populated source reference and provider identity and round-trips through the event log; removing the field from the constructor fails the test. | v11.6 p3 §1.3 External World Data Is Pass-Through; Knowledge Is Durable |
| R12-DATA-1031 | P0 | contract | **Source manifest persists retrieval timestamp and version/etag** — Each processed external source persists the retrieval timestamp and, when available, the version/etag. |  | unit: A reference built from a real fetch digest carries a populated retrieval timestamp and version/etag and round-trips through the event log; removing the field from the constructor fails the test. | v11.6 p3 §1.3 External World Data Is Pass-Through; Knowledge Is Durable |
| R12-DATA-1032 | P0 | contract | **Source manifest persists cryptographic hash/fingerprint** — Each processed external source persists a cryptographic hash/fingerprint of the bytes read. |  | unit: A reference built from a real fetch digest carries a populated cryptographic hash/fingerprint and round-trips through the event log; removing the field from the constructor fails the test. | v11.6 p3 §1.3 External World Data Is Pass-Through; Knowledge Is Durable |
| R12-DATA-1033 | P0 | contract | **Source manifest persists entitlement and geography** — Each processed external source persists its entitlement and geography. |  | unit: A reference built from a real fetch digest carries a populated entitlement and geography and round-trips through the event log; removing the field from the constructor fails the test. | v11.6 p3 §1.3 External World Data Is Pass-Through; Knowledge Is Durable |
| R12-DATA-1034 | P0 | contract | **Source manifest persists parser/version lineage** — Each processed external source persists parser/version lineage. |  | unit: A reference built from a real fetch digest carries a populated parser/version lineage and round-trips through the event log; removing the field from the constructor fails the test. | v11.6 p3 §1.3 External World Data Is Pass-Through; Knowledge Is Durable |
| R12-DATA-1035 | P0 | contract | **Source manifest persists freshness/expiry metadata** — Each processed external source persists freshness/expiry metadata. |  | unit: A reference built from a real fetch digest carries a populated freshness/expiry metadata and round-trips through the event log; removing the field from the constructor fails the test. | v11.6 p3 §1.3 External World Data Is Pass-Through; Knowledge Is Durable |
| R12-DATA-1036 | P1 | capability | **Persist compact derived knowledge** — Persist compact knowledge: normalized facts, entities, relationships, events, causal edges, embeddings/features, summaries, beliefs, confidence, contradictions, novelty, timestamps, world-model deltas and memory episodes. |  | integration: Derived knowledge records survive restart via the event log while source bodies do not. | v11.6 p4 §1.3 External World Data Is Pass-Through; Knowledge Is Durable |
| R12-DATA-1037 | P1 | process | **Historical reasoning re-fetches through the manifest** — Historical reasoning re-fetches the original source or licensed historical API using its manifest. |  | integration: A historical query re-fetches through the manifest locator and compares hashes; a revised extent is flagged. | v11.6 p4 §1.3 External World Data Is Pass-Through; Knowledge Is Durable |
| R12-DATA-1039 | P0 | constraint | **Tick and own financial records are separate retention classes** — High-rate tick/order-book data and Algorik's own financial/execution records are separate retention classes, retained only when venue rights, legal obligations and policy allow. |  | unit: The lake partitions by class and entitlement; raw venue bytes without retention entitlement are stored as hash only. | v11.6 p4 §1.3 External World Data Is Pass-Through; Knowledge Is Durable |
| R12-DATA-1041 | P2 | constraint | **Durable knowledge layer smaller than source data** — The durable knowledge layer is structured graph state, compact knowledge packs, vectors/features, summaries and episodic memory rather than copied documents or web archives, and is intentionally cheaper and smaller than source data. | `NEW_DEPENDENCY` | inspection: No durable store holds document bodies; knowledge packs serialize compactly. | v11.6 p4 §1.3 External World Data Is Pass-Through; Knowledge Is Durable |

| R12-DATA-1103 | P1 | capability | **Scout Fabric validates access rights** — The global Scout Fabric validates access rights. |  | integration: A scout run validates access rights and records the result in the event log; disabling it changes the recorded outcome. | v11.6 p5 §2 Corrections Required to v10.1 |
| R12-DATA-1104 | P2 | capability | **Scout Fabric creates adapters** — The global Scout Fabric creates adapters. |  | integration: A scout run creates adapters and records the result in the event log; disabling it changes the recorded outcome. | v11.6 p5 §2 Corrections Required to v10.1 |
| R12-DATA-1105 | P2 | capability | **Scout Fabric measures source alpha** — The global Scout Fabric measures source alpha. |  | integration: A scout run measures source alpha and records the result in the event log; disabling it changes the recorded outcome. | v11.6 p5 §2 Corrections Required to v10.1 |
| R12-DATA-1106 | P2 | capability | **Scout Fabric retires weak feeds** — The global Scout Fabric retires weak feeds. |  | integration: A scout run retires weak feeds and records the result in the event log; disabling it changes the recorded outcome. | v11.6 p5 §2 Corrections Required to v10.1 |
| R12-DATA-1153 | P2 | capability | **Global sensing capability scope** — The platform provides Global sensing with the required scope: Discovery and ingestion across web/API/WebSocket/file/stream/database/registry/satellite/geospatial/IoT/shipping/customs/blockchain/news/social/product/filing sources. |  | inspection: Each scope item of 'Global sensing' maps to code reached from a composition root with a named test; a missing item fails inspection. | v11.6 p6 §3 Full Capability Map |
| R12-DATA-2001 | P2 | capability | **Scout discovers new lawful sources without a human adding them** — The Scout Fabric runs continuously and finds new candidate sources on its own; it does not depend on a human adding each feed. |  | integration: A discovery pass with an empty operator list still produces new candidate sources from discovery targets; fails if candidates come only from a committed list. | v11.6 p12 §6 Global Scout & Data Acquisition Fabric |
| R12-DATA-2002 | P2 | capability | **Scout creates or proposes an adapter for an approved source** — For a source it approves, the Scout creates, or proposes for review, a feed adapter that can ingest it. |  | unit: A registered source yields an ingestion descriptor/adapter plan matching its access mechanism; fails if registration produces no plan. | v11.6 p12 §6 Global Scout & Data Acquisition Fabric |
| R12-DATA-2003 | P0 | process | **Access and terms of registered sources are re-checked continuously** — The Scout re-reads access method and legal terms of every registered source on each pass and stops using a source whose terms or access no longer permit it. |  | integration: Re-assessing a registered source whose robots/terms turned against it quarantines it and removes it from the catalogue; fails if it stays registered. | v11.6 p12 §6 Global Scout & Data Acquisition Fabric |
| R12-DATA-2004 | P1 | capability | **Raw market events feed reflex path and tick fabric** — Raw market events feed both the live reflex path and the historical Tick Learning Fabric, subject to venue entitlements and retention rules. |  | integration: One decoded market event reaches both the cell's pass and the tick lake with its entitlement; fails if either sink is not reached. | v11.6 p12 §6.1 Source Universe |
| R12-DATA-2005 | P1 | capability | **Exchange and market-structure sources are acquirable** — The source universe includes exchange feeds, order books, trades, quotes, options surfaces, funding, borrow, rates and venue status. | `EXTERNAL_ACTION` | inspection: Each named source class has at least one admitted connector or a documented refusal; fails if a class has neither. | v11.6 p12 §6.1 Source Universe |

| R12-DATA-2007 | P2 | capability | **News, public web and public social sources are acquirable** — The source universe includes news, press releases, expert publications, public web, deep-web databases, public social signals and public discussion communities. |  | inspection: Each named source class has at least one admitted connector or a documented refusal; fails if a class has neither. | v11.6 p12 §6.1 Source Universe |
| R12-DATA-2008 | P2 | capability | **Blockchain, mempool and protocol sources are acquirable** — The source universe includes blockchain state, mempools, token flows, smart-contract events and protocol governance. |  | inspection: Each named source class has at least one admitted connector or a documented refusal; fails if a class has neither. | v11.6 p12 §6.1 Source Universe |
| R12-DATA-2009 | P2 | capability | **Geospatial, weather, shipping and physical-flow sources are acquirable** — The source universe includes geospatial/satellite, weather, shipping/AIS, flight, port, traffic, commodity flows, energy grids and public IoT where licensed/available. | `EXTERNAL_ACTION` | inspection: Each named source class has at least one admitted connector or a documented refusal; fails if a class has neither. | v11.6 p12 §6.1 Source Universe |
| R12-DATA-2010 | P3 | capability | **Product, SKU and marketplace sources are acquirable** — The source universe includes product/SKU listings, inventories, prices, shipping estimates, marketplace fees, auction data and resale markets. |  | inspection: Each named source class has at least one admitted connector or a documented refusal; fails if a class has neither. | v11.6 p12 §6.1 Source Universe |
| R12-DATA-2011 | P0 | constraint | **Private and user-authorised datasets only under explicit entitlement** — Private, alternative, licensed and user-authorised datasets are acquired only under an explicit entitlement recorded with the source. | `EXTERNAL_ACTION` | unit: Constructing an event or registration without an entitlement is refused; fails if a default grant is substituted. | v11.6 p12 §6.1 Source Universe |
| R12-DATA-2012 | P1 | contract | **Acquisition runs fetch-parse-evidence-extract-delta-world model** — Acquisition proceeds SOURCE -> transient fetch -> parse/extract -> evidence check -> entity/event/fact/feature -> knowledge delta -> world models/memory. |  | integration: A connector poll produces records that pass schema/event gates, become typed records and update the world model; fails if a gate can be bypassed. | v11.6 p12 §6.3 Pass-Through Acquisition and Knowledge Distillation |
| R12-DATA-2013 | P0 | invariant | **Every acquisition records source manifest, content hash and cursor** — Each acquisition records a source manifest, a hash of exactly the bytes read, and the cursor/position reached. |  | integration: A poll yields a digest whose hash matches the fetched bytes and a checkpoint that resumes to the same cursor; fails if the hash covers mapped records instead of bytes. | v11.6 p12 §6.3 Pass-Through Acquisition and Knowledge Distillation |
| R12-DATA-2014 | P1 | constraint | **Raw external content is discarded after extraction** — External source payloads exist only long enough to parse, validate, extract and reason; raw external content is then discarded and durable storage begins at the knowledge boundary. |  | unit: A canary string in a fetched body appears in no journal, released record or digest after a poll; fails if any durable artefact holds the body. | v11.6 p12 §6.3 Pass-Through Acquisition and Knowledge Distillation |
| R12-DATA-2015 | P2 | process | **Discovery starts from world-model gaps and forecast errors** — The discovery loop observes gaps in the world model, strategies and forecast errors and turns them into discovery targets. |  | integration: A forecast-error spike on an uncovered entity yields a target naming it and the covering candidate is assessed first; fails if target order ignores the spike. | v11.6 p12 §6.2 Discovery Agent Loop |
| R12-DATA-2016 | P2 | capability | **Discovery generates queries by geography, entity, domain and language** — From each target the loop generates source-discovery queries and candidate locations across geography, entity, domain and language. |  | unit: queries_for yields one query per geography x domain x language and refuses a profile missing a dimension; fails if a dimension is defaulted. | v11.6 p12 §6.2 Discovery Agent Loop |
| R12-DATA-2017 | P0 | invariant | **Law and terms are inspected before a source is used** — Before registration each candidate's law/terms (licence, robots, jurisdiction) are inspected; unknown or forbidden legality is never collected regardless of score. |  | unit: A candidate scoring 1.0 everywhere with a forbidden or unknown licence is Rejected; fails if Routing can be built from score alone. | v11.6 p12 §6.2 Discovery Agent Loop |
| R12-DATA-2018 | P1 | process | **Candidate access, schema, freshness and cost are inspected** — Each candidate's access method, schema, freshness and cost are inspected from probe evidence before registration. |  | unit: A registered source's trail records a finding for each inspection attribute; fails if any attribute is unrecorded. | v11.6 p12 §6.2 Discovery Agent Loop |
| R12-DATA-2019 | P1 | process | **Candidate uniqueness and manipulation risk are inspected** — Each candidate's expected uniqueness and manipulation risk are assessed, and a source showing manipulation evidence is not registered. |  | unit: A candidate whose records are dated after the probe is refused on manipulation risk; fails if freshness rewards the future-dated payload. | v11.6 p13 §6.2 Discovery Agent Loop |
| R12-DATA-2020 | P1 | process | **Approved source metadata is registered with a named operator** — Approved source metadata is registered in a catalogue; the registration carries the legality assessment and a named operator. |  | unit: Registration without a legal assessment or with a blank operator is refused; fails if a catalogue entry exists without either. | v11.6 p13 §6.2 Discovery Agent Loop |
| R12-DATA-2021 | P1 | process | **A new source's adapter runs in a sandbox before promotion** — A newly registered source's feed/adapter is instantiated in a sandbox, and its output reaches knowledge stores only after a recorded promotion. |  | integration: A sandboxed source's records do not reach the world model until a promotion event; fails if sandbox output is ingested. | v11.6 p13 §6.2 Discovery Agent Loop |
| R12-DATA-2022 | P1 | metric | **Source scored on incremental information gain** — A source is scored on incremental information gain over the sources already held. |  | unit: The named score changes in the expected direction for a duplicate vs informative (or late vs early, flaky vs stable) source; fails if the score ignores its input. | v11.6 p13 §6.2 Discovery Agent Loop |
| R12-DATA-2023 | P1 | metric | **Source scored on timeliness** — A source is scored on timeliness, measured as how far its facts lead the same fact elsewhere. |  | unit: The named score changes in the expected direction for a duplicate vs informative (or late vs early, flaky vs stable) source; fails if the score ignores its input. | v11.6 p13 §6.2 Discovery Agent Loop |
| R12-DATA-2024 | P1 | metric | **Source scored on reliability** — A source is scored on reliability from observed availability, errors and latency. |  | unit: The named score changes in the expected direction for a duplicate vs informative (or late vs early, flaky vs stable) source; fails if the score ignores its input. | v11.6 p13 §6.2 Discovery Agent Loop |
| R12-DATA-2025 | P2 | metric | **Source scored on realised strategy contribution** — A source is scored on its realised contribution to strategy outcomes once used. |  | unit: The named score changes in the expected direction for a duplicate vs informative (or late vs early, flaky vs stable) source; fails if the score ignores its input. | v11.6 p13 §6.2 Discovery Agent Loop |
| R12-DATA-2026 | P1 | process | **Sources promoted automatically under policy** — Policy automatically promotes a registered source on re-assessment evidence, with no operator step, and journals the transition. |  | integration: A re-assessment pass whose evidence warrants it yields a promote transition journaled in the event log; fails if the registry is unchanged. | v11.6 p13 §6.2 Discovery Agent Loop |
| R12-DATA-2027 | P1 | process | **Sources throttled automatically under policy** — Policy automatically throttles a registered source on re-assessment evidence, with no operator step, and journals the transition. |  | integration: A re-assessment pass whose evidence warrants it yields a throttle transition journaled in the event log; fails if the registry is unchanged. | v11.6 p13 §6.2 Discovery Agent Loop |
| R12-DATA-2028 | P1 | process | **Sources quarantined automatically under policy** — Policy automatically quarantines a registered source on re-assessment evidence, with no operator step, and journals the transition. |  | integration: A re-assessment pass whose evidence warrants it yields a quarantine transition journaled in the event log; fails if the registry is unchanged. | v11.6 p13 §6.2 Discovery Agent Loop |
| R12-DATA-2029 | P1 | process | **Sources retired automatically under policy** — Policy automatically retires a registered source on re-assessment evidence, with no operator step, and journals the transition. |  | integration: A re-assessment pass whose evidence warrants it yields a retire transition journaled in the event log; fails if the registry is unchanged. | v11.6 p13 §6.2 Discovery Agent Loop |
| R12-DATA-3008 | P1 | capability | **Cognitive Core requests new data** — The Cognitive Core can request new data where it is blind or wrong, producing source-discovery targets. |  | integration: Forecast-error spikes and knowledge gaps produce ranked discovery targets; removing the forecast-error input empties the targets. | v11.6 p15 §9 Financial Superintelligence / Cognitive Core |
| R12-DATA-3080 | P1 | constraint | **NOW Brain persists compact state and belief deltas only** — The NOW Brain persists compact state and belief deltas, not raw world payloads. |  | unit: Persisted NOW records contain only deltas; a raw payload field fails the schema. | v11.6 p19 §9.6 Global State Estimation Engine - NOW Brain |
| R12-DATA-3105 | P2 | capability | **Information requests trigger many acquisition kinds** — Information requests may trigger a new source query, alternative provider, microstructure probe, simulation, specialist agent, public filing fetch or additional measurement. | `EXTERNAL_ACTION` | unit: Each request kind dispatches to its handler. | v11.6 p20 §9.10 Active Sensing, Curiosity & Value-of-Information |
| R12-DATA-6006 | P2 | metric | **Data-source alpha attribution after cost and delay** — For each data source, measure whether it improved decisions after its cost and its delay. |  | unit: A duplicate source scores near-zero gain; a late source's contribution is discounted by its delay; a costly one by cost. | v11.6 p32 §22 Continuous Learning & Evaluation Brain |
| R12-DATA-7013 | P2 | constraint | **Cloud Storage holds artifacts, manifests, packages, simulation outputs** — Cloud Storage holds temporary/retained artifacts, manifests, model packages, simulation outputs and reproducible campaign datasets. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | plan: Buckets exist for each named artifact class with retention set. | v11.6 p40 §26 Platform & Deployment Architecture |
| R12-DATA-7021 | P0 | invariant | **External world content refetched, never retained as raw corpus** — External world source content is re-fetched from its origin/provider when needed and is never retained as a raw corpus. |  | acceptance: No store persists raw external documents; retention keeps only derived records and refetch pointers. | v11.6 p40 §26 Platform & Deployment Architecture; v11.6 p51 §33 Final Definition of Algorik |

| R12-EVID-1038 | P1 | invariant | **Retain knowledge and mark evidence no longer re-fetchable** — If the original cannot be retrieved, the derived knowledge is retained together with the fact that the evidence is no longer re-fetchable. |  | unit: A 404 on re-fetch yields an Unretrievable marker on the extent while the derived knowledge remains readable. | v11.6 p4 §1.3 External World Data Is Pass-Through; Knowledge Is Durable |
| R12-EVID-1107 | P1 | capability | **Evidence/Truth Fabric provides provenance graph** — The Evidence/Truth Fabric provides provenance graph. |  | unit: A conflicting or forged input is detected by the provenance graph mechanism and the finding recorded; removing it lets the input through. | v11.6 p5 §2 Corrections Required to v10.1 |
| R12-EVID-1108 | P1 | capability | **Evidence/Truth Fabric provides corroboration** — The Evidence/Truth Fabric provides corroboration. |  | unit: A conflicting or forged input is detected by the corroboration mechanism and the finding recorded; removing it lets the input through. | v11.6 p5 §2 Corrections Required to v10.1 |
| R12-EVID-1109 | P1 | capability | **Evidence/Truth Fabric provides contradiction resolution** — The Evidence/Truth Fabric provides contradiction resolution. |  | unit: A conflicting or forged input is detected by the contradiction resolution mechanism and the finding recorded; removing it lets the input through. | v11.6 p5 §2 Corrections Required to v10.1 |
| R12-EVID-1110 | P1 | capability | **Evidence/Truth Fabric provides cryptographic/source authenticity** — The Evidence/Truth Fabric provides cryptographic/source authenticity. |  | unit: A conflicting or forged input is detected by the cryptographic/source authenticity mechanism and the finding recorded; removing it lets the input through. | v11.6 p5 §2 Corrections Required to v10.1 |
| R12-EVID-1111 | P1 | capability | **Evidence/Truth Fabric provides time/location consistency** — The Evidence/Truth Fabric provides time/location consistency. |  | unit: A conflicting or forged input is detected by the time/location consistency mechanism and the finding recorded; removing it lets the input through. | v11.6 p5 §2 Corrections Required to v10.1 |
| R12-EVID-1112 | P1 | capability | **Evidence/Truth Fabric provides adversarial-source detection** — The Evidence/Truth Fabric provides adversarial-source detection. |  | unit: A conflicting or forged input is detected by the adversarial-source detection mechanism and the finding recorded; removing it lets the input through. | v11.6 p5 §2 Corrections Required to v10.1 |
| R12-EVID-1154 | P1 | capability | **Evidence & trust capability scope** — The platform provides Evidence & trust with the required scope: Source identity, provenance, licensing, freshness, corroboration, contradictions, manipulation detection, confidence and audit trail. |  | inspection: Each scope item of 'Evidence & trust' maps to code reached from a composition root with a named test; a missing item fails inspection. | v11.6 p6 §3 Full Capability Map |
| R12-EVID-1200 | P0 | invariant | **Scout -> Evidence & Truth -> World model ordering** — World data passes through the global scout, then evidence & truth, before reaching the world model/causal memory. |  | integration: A record failing its quality gate is refused by name and never reaches the world model. | v11.6 p10 §5 End-to-End System Path |
| R12-EVID-2030 | P0 | invariant | **No single source is truth; beliefs update from evidence** — Extracted claims update beliefs through evidence weighting; no single source and no scraped text is converted directly into a fact. |  | unit: No finite evidence drives a belief to certainty and contradicting evidence lowers it; fails if a single item sets the belief to 1. | v11.6 p13 §7 Evidence, Provenance & Truth Fabric |
| R12-EVID-2031 | P0 | contract | **Every extracted claim becomes an Evidence object with full provenance** — Every extracted claim becomes an Evidence object carrying source identity, timestamp, location, extraction method, authenticity signals, license and confidence. |  | unit: Constructing evidence without source, extraction method or license is refused, and every ingested world claim is wrapped; fails if a claim reaches the world model without one. | v11.6 p13 §7 Evidence, Provenance & Truth Fabric |
| R12-EVID-2032 | P1 | contract | **Evidence links to corroborating and contradicting evidence** — Each Evidence object carries links to the evidence that corroborates or contradicts it. |  | unit: Linking two evidence items records the relation on both sides and survives replay; fails if links are dropped. | v11.6 p13 §7 Evidence, Provenance & Truth Fabric |
| R12-EVID-2033 | P1 | capability | **A provenance graph of evidence is built** — The system builds a provenance graph linking claims to sources and to each other. |  | integration: From a belief, walking the graph reaches every source document hash it rests on; fails if any edge is missing. | v11.6 p13 §7 Evidence, Provenance & Truth Fabric |
| R12-EVID-2034 | P1 | capability | **Authenticity signals are recorded and checked** — Authenticity is assessed from TLS/domain identity, signed releases where available, document hashes, chain proofs, known issuer identity and source history. |  | unit: Evidence from a verified-hash, registered-issuer source outranks an unsigned copy; fails if authenticity has no effect on weight. | v11.6 p13 §7 Evidence, Provenance & Truth Fabric |
| R12-EVID-2035 | P0 | invariant | **Corroboration discounts common-source dependence** — Corroboration is weighted by independent-source agreement so that copies of one origin (ten copies of one press release) count as one source. |  | property: Adding copies of an existing origin leaves supporting weight unchanged while a new origin raises it; fails if a per-copy increment is reintroduced. | v11.6 p13 §7 Evidence, Provenance & Truth Fabric |
| R12-EVID-2036 | P1 | process | **Claims are checked for temporal consistency** — Each claim is checked against prior state, event ordering and physically possible timelines, and the result is recorded. |  | unit: A claim dated after its publication or before its declared cause is marked inconsistent; fails if marked Consistent. | v11.6 p13 §7 Evidence, Provenance & Truth Fabric |
| R12-EVID-2037 | P2 | process | **Claims are checked for geospatial consistency** — Location, routing, shipping, weather and jurisdiction evidence must agree where relevant, and disagreement is recorded. |  | unit: Two claims placing one entity in incompatible locations are marked inconsistent; fails if no check runs. | v11.6 p13 §7 Evidence, Provenance & Truth Fabric |
| R12-EVID-2038 | P1 | capability | **Contradictions kept as competing hypotheses until resolved** — Conflicting evidence is not deleted; competing hypotheses are maintained and the later evidence that resolves them is tracked. |  | unit: A contradiction is recorded with both sides retained and later resolved with the resolving evidence named; fails if either side is removed. | v11.6 p13 §7 Evidence, Provenance & Truth Fabric |
| R12-EVID-2039 | P0 | capability | **Adversarial and poisoned sources are detected** — The fabric detects spam, coordinated manipulation, synthetic content, poisoned feeds, spoofing and anomalous source behaviour. |  | integration: A planted coordinated campaign (same timing, near-identical text across origins) is flagged; fails if the detector returns no finding. | v11.6 p13 §7 Evidence, Provenance & Truth Fabric |
| R12-EVID-2040 | P3 | constraint | **Quantum methods never confer authenticity or truth** — Quantum optimisation/clustering on evidence graphs is optional and experimental, and no quantum output may set authenticity or truth. |  | inspection: No path from a quantum result type to an authenticity or belief field; fails if one compiles. | v11.6 p13 §7 Evidence, Provenance & Truth Fabric |
| R12-EVID-3081 | P1 | constraint | **External evidence re-fetchable by manifest** — External evidence behind NOW state remains re-fetchable by manifest rather than stored. |  | unit: Each NOW delta cites a manifest entry resolvable to a source reference. | v11.6 p19 §9.6 Global State Estimation Engine - NOW Brain |
| R12-EVID-7010 | P0 | invariant | **Append-only object/event archive holds immutable evidence** — Beside the ledger store, an append-only object/event archive holds immutable evidence. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | integration: Archive objects are write-once (retention lock/hash chain); an overwrite attempt is refused. | v11.6 p39 §26 Platform & Deployment Architecture |

| R12-TICK-2049 | P0 | contract | **Capture keeps source sequence, exchange time and receive time** — Venue capture adapters decode native feeds preserving source sequence numbers, exchange timestamps and receive timestamps. |  | unit: A decoded event exposes venue sequence, exchange time and receive time as distinct values; fails if any is collapsed. | v11.6 p14 §8.2 Tick Data Pipeline and Storage |
| R12-TICK-2050 | P1 | capability | **Raw venue messages preserved where rights allow** — Capture preserves raw venue messages where rights allow, and keeps the payload hash where they do not. |  | unit: A permitting entitlement stores raw bytes and a non-permitting one stores only the hash; fails if bytes are stored without rights. | v11.6 p14 §8.2 Tick Data Pipeline and Storage |
| R12-TICK-2051 | P0 | invariant | **Event, receive and normalized time and uncertainty stay distinct** — Normalisation preserves event time, receive time, sequence, venue and clock uncertainty as distinct fields rather than collapsing them to one timestamp. |  | unit: Round-tripping a canonical event preserves all three times and the uncertainty; fails if any is overwritten. | v11.6 p14 §8.2 Tick Data Pipeline and Storage |
| R12-TICK-2052 | P3 | capability | **PTP/GPS-grade clock discipline where justified** — Capture nodes use PTP/GPS-grade clock discipline where the strategy justifies it, and publish the resulting uncertainty. | `MULTI_REGION` | game-day: A disciplined node's published uncertainty bounds its measured offset against a reference; fails if offset exceeds the bound. | v11.6 p14 §8.2 Tick Data Pipeline and Storage |
| R12-TICK-2053 | P1 | contract | **Canonical typed market-event schema for all event kinds** — Trades, quotes, depth updates, auctions, status and lifecycle events normalise into typed canonical records. |  | unit: Each event kind decodes to its typed variant; fails if any kind lands in a generic bucket. | v11.6 p14 §8.2 Tick Data Pipeline and Storage |
| R12-TICK-2054 | P0 | invariant | **Canonical records retain source payload hash and lineage** — Every canonical record retains the source-native payload hash and lineage. |  | unit: Constructing a MarketEvent with a hash for a different payload is refused; fails if the hash is trusted. | v11.6 p14 §8.2 Tick Data Pipeline and Storage |
| R12-TICK-2055 | P0 | invariant | **Immutable, checksummed, partitioned Tick Lake with manifests** — The Tick Lake stores partitioned historical raw/canonical events immutably, with checksums and reproducible manifests. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | integration: A sealed segment cannot be overwritten and a manifest re-materialises to matching checksums; fails on any accepted overwrite or unflagged corrupt byte. | v11.6 p14 §8.2 Tick Data Pipeline and Storage |
| R12-TICK-2056 | P0 | invariant | **Tick Lake partitions enforce entitlement and retention** — Lake partitions carry their entitlement and retention class, and a job reads only partitions it is entitled to. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | unit: A job granted one entitlement materialises only that partition; market-class objects have a delete lifecycle and internal-class never deletes; fails otherwise. | v11.6 p14 §8.2 Tick Data Pipeline and Storage |
| R12-TICK-2057 | P2 | capability | **Tick Lake compresses segments** — Lake segments are compressed. | `NEW_DEPENDENCY` `COST` | unit: A written segment is smaller than its input and round-trips exactly; fails if stored uncompressed. | v11.6 p14 §8.2 Tick Data Pipeline and Storage |
| R12-TICK-2058 | P2 | capability | **Hot replay indexes separated from cold archive** — Hot replay indexes are kept separate from the cold archive so replay can seek without scanning archives. |  | unit: Reading record N uses the manifest's index and fetches only its byte range; fails if the whole object is read. | v11.6 p14 §8.2 Tick Data Pipeline and Storage |
| R12-TICK-2059 | P1 | invariant | **Order books rebuild deterministically with checkpoints** — Venue books are rebuilt deterministically from canonical events, with snapshots/checkpoints. |  | acceptance: Replaying one session twice and from a checkpoint yields identical books, fills and flags; fails on any divergence. | v11.6 p14 §8.2 Tick Data Pipeline and Storage |
| R12-TICK-2060 | P0 | invariant | **Reconstruction detects gaps and out-of-order events** — Book reconstruction detects sequence gaps and out-of-order events. |  | unit: Removing one message or reordering one beyond the window is detected; fails if the book is published. | v11.6 p14 §8.2 Tick Data Pipeline and Storage |
| R12-TICK-2061 | P0 | invariant | **Unreliable reconstruction periods are marked on output** — Periods where reconstruction is unreliable are marked on the replay output. |  | unit: A gap yields an UnreliablePeriod on the output until the next Reset; fails if the span is unflagged. | v11.6 p14 §8.2 Tick Data Pipeline and Storage |
| R12-TICK-2062 | P1 | invariant | **Cross-venue alignment preserves uncertainty without artificial simultaneity** — Asynchronous instruments are aligned by event time and information arrival, preserving uncertainty and never asserting an order the clocks cannot support. |  | property: Events within combined uncertainty are reported unordered; fails if the aligner orders them. | v11.6 p14 §8.2 Tick Data Pipeline and Storage |
| R12-TICK-2063 | P1 | capability | **Microstructure feature engine computes the named feature families** — The feature engine generates spread, depth, imbalance, queue, flow, toxicity, volatility, liquidity, lead/lag and cross-venue features. |  | unit: Each named family has a feature with a hand-computed test; fails if any family is absent. | v11.6 p14 §8.2 Tick Data Pipeline and Storage |
| R12-TICK-2064 | P3 | capability | **Learned embedding representations of market state** — The representation engine produces learned embeddings of market state. |  | unit: An embedding model is trained, versioned and evaluated against a baseline; fails if no artefact. | v11.6 p14 §8.2 Tick Data Pipeline and Storage |
| R12-TICK-2065 | P1 | capability | **Outcome labels built for fill, return, selection, impact, vol and decay** — The label builder creates fill-probability, future-return, adverse-selection, market-impact, realised-volatility, opportunity-decay and multi-horizon outcome labels. |  | unit: Each label matches a hand computation; fails on a sign or window error. | v11.6 p14 §8.2 Tick Data Pipeline and Storage |
| R12-TICK-2066 | P0 | invariant | **Labels are built without future leakage** — Labels are never available as inputs at or before the instant they describe, and a window the series does not cover is refused rather than shortened. |  | unit: A horizon past the series end is refused; a dataset whose feature timestamp exceeds its label anchor is refused; fails if accepted. | v11.6 p14 §8.2 Tick Data Pipeline and Storage |
| R12-TICK-2067 | P1 | capability | **Replay engine replays history and decisions with execution realism** — The replay/simulation engine replays canonical historical markets and the platform's decisions with latency, queue, fees, rejects, partial fills, impact and multi-leg execution semantics. |  | integration: A decision replayed against history incurs latency, fees, partial fill and impact; fails if any is zeroed. | v11.6 p14 §8.2 Tick Data Pipeline and Storage |
| R12-TICK-2068 | P2 | capability | **Microstructure models are trained and calibrated** — A training service trains/calibrates specialist, execution, liquidity, slippage, fill, regime and short-horizon models. |  | integration: A fill-probability model trained on labelled ticks beats its baseline on holdout; fails if no such model is produced. | v11.6 p14 §8.2 Tick Data Pipeline and Storage |
| R12-TICK-2069 | P2 | metric | **Microstructure models benchmarked by venue, asset, regime and horizon** — Microstructure models are benchmarked separately by venue, asset, regime and horizon. |  | unit: An evaluation report has one row per venue x asset x regime x horizon; fails if aggregated. | v11.6 p14 §8.2 Tick Data Pipeline and Storage |
| R12-TICK-2070 | P1 | capability | **Slow intelligence distilled into bounded reflex artefacts** — Slow/warm intelligence is distilled into bounded features, models or lookup structures that reflex nodes evaluate within their latency budgets. |  | integration: A distilled student has a stated worst-case cost and fidelity to its teacher and loads in the edge cell; fails if unbounded or unfaithful. | v11.6 p14 §8.2 Tick Data Pipeline and Storage |
| R12-TICK-2071 | P1 | metric | **Online drift measures residuals, fill error and venue change** — Live residuals, fill/slippage error, regime drift and venue changes are measured continuously. |  | integration: A shifted feature distribution raises the drift score and the model card becomes ineligible; fails if the score stays zero. | v11.6 p14 §8.2 Tick Data Pipeline and Storage |
| R12-TICK-2072 | P0 | invariant | **Drift feeds retraining, never silent adaptation of money-moving logic** — Drift and feedback feed retraining and curriculum tasks; money-moving logic is never silently adapted. |  | unit: An update outside its cadence's permitted scope is refused; fails if a fast update changes model structure. | v11.6 p14 §8.2 Tick Data Pipeline and Storage |
| R12-TICK-2073 | P0 | invariant | **Strategies evaluated against the market that existed** — A strategy is evaluated against the recorded market that existed, never against bars constructed after the fact. |  | unit: A decision view cannot read a bar before its close time; fails if a same-day bar is visible intraday. | v11.6 p14 §8.3 Market Replay, Counterfactual Execution and Synthetic Experience |
| R12-TICK-2074 | P1 | capability | **Replay models data arrival and decision latency** — The simulator models what the platform could have known at each instant, including data arrival delays and decision latency. |  | unit: Delayed-feed and latency conditions push the execution later and worsen fills; fails if they have no effect. | v11.6 p14 §8.3 Market Replay, Counterfactual Execution and Synthetic Experience |
| R12-TICK-2075 | P1 | capability | **Replay models venue rate limits** — The simulator enforces venue rate limits on replayed order flow. |  | unit: Orders beyond the venue's message budget are refused in replay; fails if they fill. | v11.6 p14 §8.3 Market Replay, Counterfactual Execution and Synthetic Experience |
| R12-TICK-2076 | P0 | invariant | **Exact historical replay with checkpoints and integrity flags** — Exact historical replay sequences events deterministically from venue-native/canonical records with book checkpoints and integrity flags. |  | acceptance: Same session replayed twice and from a checkpoint gives identical event order, books, fills and flags; fails on any difference. | v11.6 p15 §8.3 Market Replay, Counterfactual Execution and Synthetic Experience |
| R12-TICK-2077 | P1 | capability | **Counterfactual replay of taken, vetoed and missed actions** — Actions taken, vetoed or missed are compared against feasible alternatives using only the information set available at that time. |  | integration: A refused order is priced once its horizon passes against a view fixed at decision time; fails if the alternative reads later data. | v11.6 p15 §8.3 Market Replay, Counterfactual Execution and Synthetic Experience |

| R12-TICK-2079 | P2 | capability | **Agent/market simulation generates rare regimes and stress** — Agent/market simulation generates synthetic rare regimes, liquidity droughts, manipulation patterns, venue outages and stress events. |  | unit: Each named stress class can be generated deterministically from a seed; fails if a class is unavailable. | v11.6 p15 §8.3 Market Replay, Counterfactual Execution and Synthetic Experience |
| R12-TICK-2080 | P1 | process | **Execution digital twin continuously calibrated to live telemetry** — Fill, queue, impact and latency models of the execution twin are continuously calibrated to the platform's own telemetry. |  | integration: After fills with systematic error the twin's model parameters move toward the observed fills; fails if parameters are static. | v11.6 p15 §8.3 Market Replay, Counterfactual Execution and Synthetic Experience |

| R12-TICK-2087 | P0 | invariant | **No look-ahead: only data observable by the decision instant** — Training and replay use only data observable by the decision timestamp, including realistic publication and feed-arrival delays. |  | property: Any read at decision time t returns only records with known_at <= t (with delay); fails if a later record is visible. | v11.6 p15 §8.5 Data Correctness and Training Guardrails |
| R12-TICK-2088 | P0 | invariant | **No impossible fills in replay** — A replayed trade respects available depth, queue/priority, rate limits, latency, partial fills, venue rules and price impact. |  | unit: No replayed fill exceeds resting depth; an alternative beyond calibrated participation is unfillable; fails if either fills. | v11.6 p15 §8.5 Data Correctness and Training Guardrails |
| R12-TICK-2089 | P0 | invariant | **Clock corrections are lineage-tracked** — Source, receive and normalized time and their uncertainty remain distinct, and every clock correction is recorded in lineage. |  | unit: A corrected timestamp carries a ClockCorrection record that survives into the stored event; fails if the correction is lost. | v11.6 p15 §8.5 Data Correctness and Training Guardrails |
| R12-TICK-2090 | P0 | invariant | **No hidden survivorship in historical universes** — Delisted instruments, venue outages, symbol changes, corporate actions and failed products remain represented in historical universes. |  | unit: members_as_of a past date includes an instrument delisted later; a replay across an outage reports it; fails otherwise. | v11.6 p15 §8.5 Data Correctness and Training Guardrails |
| R12-TICK-2091 | P0 | invariant | **Model artefacts record training-data entitlements and permitted use** — Model artefacts record which licensed market data trained them and where the artefact and its outputs are permitted to run or be used. |  | unit: A model card carries its training entitlements and permitted locations, and deployment to a non-permitted location is refused; fails if accepted. | v11.6 p15 §8.5 Data Correctness and Training Guardrails |
| R12-TICK-2092 | P1 | process | **Tick models must prove value over simpler baselines** — Models and the Evaluation Brain must prove incremental predictive or execution value versus simpler baselines; raw tick volume is not credited by itself. |  | unit: A model whose holdout skill does not beat the baseline is refused registration as skilled; fails if registered. | v11.6 p15 §8.5 Data Correctness and Training Guardrails |

| R12-TICK-3099 | P2 | contract | **Branches carry probability, assumptions, evidence, propagation, impact, hedges** — Each future branch carries probability, causal assumptions, unresolved evidence, expected market propagation, portfolio impact and hedge requirements. |  | unit: A branch missing any of the six fields is refused. | v11.6 p19 §9.9 Future-State Tree, Synthetic Worlds & Digital Twins |
| R12-TICK-3100 | P2 | capability | **Reality prunes, reweights or splits branches** — Arriving observations continuously prune, reweight or split future branches. |  | unit: An observation consistent with one branch reweights it; a contradicting one prunes. | v11.6 p19 §9.9 Future-State Tree, Synthetic Worlds & Digital Twins |
| R12-TICK-3101 | P1 | capability | **Exchange/venue twins simulate queueing, latency, fills, slippage, impact, responses** — Exchange/venue twins simulate queueing, latency, fills, slippage, market impact and participant responses. |  | unit: SimBook sweep never fills beyond published depth; impact and latency applied. | v11.6 p20 §9.9 Future-State Tree, Synthetic Worlds & Digital Twins |
| R12-TICK-3102 | P2 | capability | **Macro/company/supply-chain twins explore second/third-order propagation** — Macro, company and supply-chain twins explore second- and third-order propagation paths before they are obvious in prices. |  | integration: Propagation names instruments two hops from a driver the book depends on. | v11.6 p20 §9.9 Future-State Tree, Synthetic Worlds & Digital Twins |
| R12-TICK-6014 | P2 | process | **Tick-learning loop reconciles live fills against replay assumptions** — Fills, slippage, queue errors, missed/expired opportunities and book-regime changes are reconciled against replay assumptions and become microstructure retraining/evaluation data. |  | integration: A fill whose price differs from the twin's assumption is charted and enters a retraining dataset. | v11.6 p32 §22 Continuous Learning & Evaluation Brain |
| R12-TICK-7019 | P1 | capability | **Tick archive in object storage with partitioning, manifests, entitlements** — Tick and internal execution history are archived in Cloud Storage/object storage with compressed partitioning, manifests and entitlements. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | integration: Archived partitions carry a manifest and entitlement; a partition without one is refused at read. | v11.6 p40 §26 Platform & Deployment Architecture |
| R12-TICK-7020 | P2 | constraint | **Replay workers on dedicated compute with local caches/indexes** — Replay workers use dedicated compute and local high-throughput caches/indexes. | `COST` | plan: A replay worker pool is provisioned separately from serving. | v11.6 p40 §26 Platform & Deployment Architecture |

## WORLD

World Model Federation, memory, self-model, specialist brains.

| R12-WORLD-9017 | P3 | constraint | **NOW state partitioned by domain and region** — NOW state is partitioned by domain and region so it scales and fails per partition. |  | unit: State keys carry domain and region; a write to one partition does not touch another. | GCP v2.1 p12 §11.2 Global State Estimation / NOW Services |
| R12-WORLD-9018 | P0 | invariant | **Stale NOW state lowers confidence and never blocks Reflex** — Stale NOW state lowers confidence and never blocks Reflex execution. |  | integration: With NOW state past its freshness bound, confidence drops and the cell still runs; making the cell wait on NOW fails the test. | GCP v2.1 p12 §11.2 Global State Estimation / NOW Services |
| R12-WORLD-9019 | P3 | capability | **state-filter-workers produce latent-state candidates; missing worker reduces diversity** — State-filter workers turn typed observations and state priors into candidate latent-state updates, and a missing worker reduces ensemble diversity rather than failing estimation. | `KUBERNETES` | unit: Removing one worker still yields an estimate with a reported smaller ensemble. | GCP v2.1 p12 §11.2 Global State Estimation / NOW Services |
| R12-WORLD-9020 | P2 | invariant | **state-reconciler is a single fenced domain leader promoting state** — A state-reconciler, run as a single domain leader with fencing, promotes state and confidence from candidate states and symbolic constraints. | `KUBERNETES` `MANAGED_SERVICE` | unit: A reconciler with a stale fencing epoch cannot promote; candidates violating a symbolic constraint are not promoted. | GCP v2.1 p12 §11.2 Global State Estimation / NOW Services |
| R12-WORLD-9021 | P2 | invariant | **Last promoted state remains valid until expiry** — When the reconciler is unavailable, the last promoted state remains valid until its expiry. |  | unit: After reconciler loss the state is served until expires_at and refused after. | GCP v2.1 p12 §11.2 Global State Estimation / NOW Services |
| R12-WORLD-9022 | P2 | capability | **forecast-router routes questions by domain/horizon/model society** — A forecast-router routes forecast questions by domain, horizon and model society using a routing/reputation cache, and falls back to default ensembles on failure. | `KUBERNETES` `MANAGED_SERVICE` | unit: A question is routed to the model with the best reputation in its context; with the cache unavailable the default ensemble answers. | GCP v2.1 p12 §11.3 Temporal Forecast Lattice Services |
| R12-WORLD-9023 | P2 | capability | **forecast-micro runs calibrated microstructure forecasts locally** — Microsecond-to-minute calibrated microstructure forecasts run locally in Reflex (with warm backup) and continue independently of central services. |  | integration: The cell evaluates a local model in its pass with no network call; central unavailability does not stop it. | GCP v2.1 p12 §11.3 Temporal Forecast Lattice Services |
| R12-WORLD-9024 | P1 | invariant | **forecast-tactical outputs expire when stale and feed capital sizing** — Minutes-to-days tactical forecasts expire when stale, and capital sizing contracts on their staleness. | `KUBERNETES` | unit: A forecast past its expiry is not used and sizing shrinks; removing the expiry check fails the test. | GCP v2.1 p12 §11.3 Temporal Forecast Lattice Services |
| R12-WORLD-9025 | P3 | invariant | **forecast-strategic outage accumulates backlog, never an execution outage** — Weeks-to-years strategic forecasts run as a separate service whose outage only accumulates research backlog and never causes an execution outage. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` | integration: Stopping the strategic service leaves execution passing. | GCP v2.1 p12 §11.3 Temporal Forecast Lattice Services |

| R12-AGENCY-3002 | P2 | capability | **Cognitive Core decomposes goals** — The Cognitive Core can decompose a goal into sub-goals, each a validated goal specification. |  | unit: Decomposing a goal yields typed sub-goals and refuses a malformed part; removing validation makes the refusal test fail. | v11.6 p15 §9 Financial Superintelligence / Cognitive Core |
| R12-AGENCY-3004 | P1 | capability | **Cognitive Core calls tools through authorized adapters** — The Cognitive Core calls tools only through registered tools with declared authority, and a plan step using an undeclared tool or unheld authority is refused. | `EXTERNAL_ACTION` | unit: A plan using a lever whose authority the identity lacks is refused; deleting the authority check fails the test. | v11.6 p15 §9 Financial Superintelligence / Cognitive Core |
| R12-AGENCY-3028 | P2 | capability | **Causal Agency Brain** — A Causal Agency Brain exists as a specialist covering: goal decomposition, controllable-variable discovery, affordance graph, intervention generation, causal simulation, multi-step planning, effect/risk/cost comparison and execution orchestration through authorized tools. |  | integration: The Causal Agency Brain is constructed in the production organisation and emits findings for its scope; removing it drops its scope from coverage. | v11.6 p16 §9.1 Specialist Brain Society |
| R12-AGENCY-6078 | P2 | capability | **Agency engine between cognition and tool execution** — The Causal Agency & Intervention Engine sits between cognition and tool execution, turning desired outcomes into testable, policy-constrained causal action plans and turning action results back into learned causal structure. |  | integration: A request flows goal -> plan -> gate -> shadow, and an attributed result updates the causal structure. | v11.6 p36 §24 Causal Agency & Intervention Intelligence |
| R12-AGENCY-6079 | P1 | contract | **Desired outcome is a typed GoalSpec with all declarations** — A desired outcome is a typed GoalSpec declaring target state/distribution, entities affected, time horizon, success metric, acceptable uncertainty, budget, risk envelope, legal/jurisdictional boundary, acting identity, prohibited side effects and stop/rollback conditions. |  | unit: A goal missing any one declaration is refused naming that field; a document missing a field is refused on deserialisation, not defaulted. | v11.6 p37 §24.1 Desired Outcomes, Utility and Constraints |
| R12-AGENCY-6080 | P2 | contract | **Goals cover eight objective classes** — Goals may be financial, operational, research, product, liquidity, risk-reduction, information-acquisition or communications objectives. |  | unit: A goal of each of the eight classes round-trips through serde unchanged. | v11.6 p37 §24.1 Desired Outcomes, Utility and Constraints |
| R12-AGENCY-6081 | P0 | invariant | **Outcome preference never implies action permission** — Wanting an outcome never implies permission to use every causal lever that might achieve it; each lever requires authority the acting identity independently holds. |  | unit: A plan using a lever whose authority the identity lacks is refused whatever its effect. | v11.6 p37 §24.1 Desired Outcomes, Utility and Constraints |
| R12-AGENCY-6082 | P1 | contract | **Action-affordance graph extends the world model federation** — The World Model Federation is extended with an action-affordance graph recording which variables can be observed and controlled, which tools affect them, expected latency/cost, causal paths, side effects, dependencies and authority requirements. |  | unit: A tool edge missing a declaration or on an observable-only variable is refused; levers are only controllable variables with a tool edge. | v11.6 p37 §24.2 Action-Affordance & Causal Lever Graph |
| R12-AGENCY-6083 | P0 | invariant | **Symbolic hard constraints; learned models estimate effect sizes** — Symbolic reasoning enforces hard constraints while learned causal models estimate uncertain effect sizes; no estimate outweighs a constraint. |  | property: No learned effect estimate however large gets a plan past a hard constraint. | v11.6 p37 §24.2 Action-Affordance & Causal Lever Graph |
| R12-AGENCY-6084 | P2 | capability | **Interventions modelled as do(action) with counterfactual branches** — Interventions are represented with structural causal models and counterfactual branches: do(action) -> intermediate states -> target outcome. |  | unit: An intervention's effect is computed by graph surgery, distinct from the observational association. | v11.6 p37 §24.2 Action-Affordance & Causal Lever Graph |
| R12-AGENCY-6085 | P0 | invariant | **Weak identification leads to abstain, gather evidence or experiment** — When causal identification is weak, the engine abstains, gathers more evidence or proposes an experiment rather than acting. |  | unit: A plan pulling a weakly identified lever is never returned for execution. | v11.6 p37 §24.2 Action-Affordance & Causal Lever Graph |
| R12-AGENCY-6086 | P1 | invariant | **Candidate set always includes a no-action baseline** — Every candidate set includes exactly one no-action baseline against which actions are judged. |  | unit: A candidate set without exactly one baseline and one alternative is refused; an effect not strictly positive loses to no action. | v11.6 p37 §24.3 Intervention Planner |
| R12-AGENCY-6087 | P2 | capability | **Planner generates multiple candidate action sequences** — The planner itself generates multiple candidate action sequences for a goal. |  | unit: Given a goal and graph with no proposals, the planner produces at least two admissible candidates. | v11.6 p37 §24.3 Intervention Planner |
| R12-AGENCY-6088 | P2 | capability | **Simulate candidates across world models, twins and scenarios** — Each candidate is simulated across competing world models, market/tick digital twins and scenario branches. |  | integration: A candidate's simulation produces one result per world model and twin scenario. | v11.6 p37 §24.3 Intervention Planner |
| R12-AGENCY-6089 | P1 | contract | **Candidates compared on nine declared axes** — Candidates are compared on expected causal effect, confidence, cost, capital usage, time-to-effect, reversibility, legal eligibility, conduct risk and downside. |  | unit: A comparison missing any axis is refused; changing conduct risk or downside changes the ranking. | v11.6 p37 §24.3 Intervention Planner |
| R12-AGENCY-6090 | P2 | contract | **Plans may be parallel, sequential, conditional or adaptive** — Candidate interventions can be parallel, sequential, conditional or adaptive, with bounds held against every branch. |  | unit: A conditional runs only when its condition holds; an adaptive switches; a bound is checked against every branch. | v11.6 p37 §24.3 Intervention Planner |
| R12-AGENCY-6091 | P0 | invariant | **Action only through registered tools with identity, permission, audit** — The platform acts only through registered tools with explicit identities, permissions and audit. |  | unit: An unregistered tool or one without identity is refused and every decision audited; the only adapter caller checks the registry. | v11.6 p37 §24.4 Authorized Action Surfaces |
