| R12-AGENCY-6099 | P0 | constraint | **Communications & Conduct Brain checks seven dimensions** — Before any external communication, check factual support, provenance, identity, required disclosures, audience/channel permissions, jurisdiction and whether the pattern could constitute deceptive coordination or manipulation. | `EXTERNAL_ACTION` | unit: A draft failing any one check is blocked; each check independently enforced. | v11.6 p38 §24.5 Communications Integrity & Anti-Manipulation Boundary |
| R12-AGENCY-6100 | P0 | invariant | **Deceptive and manipulative techniques are never used** — The platform never uses fabricated identities, sockpuppets, bots posing as people, fake consensus, false/misleading claims, undisclosed paid promotion, rumour manufacture, wash trading, spoofing, pump-and-dump or other deceptive techniques to move prices or beliefs. |  | property: No tool edge of a deceptive method reaches a plan; the order path refuses a self-trade. | v11.6 p38 §24.5 Communications Integrity & Anti-Manipulation Boundary |
| R12-AGENCY-6101 | P0 | invariant | **A plan that depends on deception is infeasible** — If a causal plan depends on deception, the plan is infeasible whatever its expected effect. |  | property: A plan with a deceptive step anywhere in it is refused for every generated effect estimate. | v11.6 p38 §24.5 Communications Integrity & Anti-Manipulation Boundary |
| R12-AGENCY-6102 | P2 | capability | **Post-intervention causal effect estimated by identified methods** — After an intervention the Evaluation Brain estimates the caused change using randomised/staged experiments where feasible, else matched/synthetic controls, difference-in-differences, instrumental/structural methods, Bayesian causal models, counterfactual branches and no-action baselines. |  | unit: Each method produces an estimate with an identification label; none exists without one. | v11.6 p38 §24.6 Experimentation and Causal Effect Attribution |
| R12-AGENCY-6103 | P1 | invariant | **Correlation never upgraded to causal certainty** — The platform records uncertainty and never upgrades correlation into causal certainty without evidence. |  | unit: A NotIdentified attribution never widens a policy. | v11.6 p38 §24.6 Experimentation and Causal Effect Attribution |
| R12-AGENCY-6104 | P1 | contract | **Effect attribution multi-horizon with chain and rivals** — Effects are measured at multiple horizons, and the attribution preserves the causal chain, confounders, competing explanations and confidence rather than a final KPI. |  | unit: An attribution without chain, confounders, rivals, confidence or horizon observations is refused. | v11.6 p38 §24.6 Experimentation and Causal Effect Attribution |
| R12-AGENCY-6105 | P1 | invariant | **Ambient signals reach agency only via Attention Router and gates** — Ambient models may surface a DesiredOutcome or ActionOpportunity, which never directly causes action: the Attention Router sends it to the Agency Brain, which builds a GoalSpec and candidates, runs simulation and policy/conduct gates and obtains the required authority. |  | integration: An ambient opportunity produces a GoalSpec and a gated, shadowed plan and no adapter call. | v11.6 p38 §24.7 Ambient Agency Loop |
| R12-AGENCY-6106 | P1 | invariant | **Agency loop runs in declared order end to end** — The loop is observe -> predict -> specify desired state -> identify levers -> generate -> simulate -> verify authority/conduct -> act -> observe -> attribute -> update world models, policies and priors. |  | unit: A stage is admitted only in order and never after a failure; the full eleven stages complete on one pass. | v11.6 p38 §24.7 Ambient Agency Loop |
| R12-AGENCY-6107 | P2 | contract | **Action Outcome Memory records nine fields** — An Action Outcome Memory records intervention context, audience/environment state, action sequence, causal hypothesis, predicted effect, actual observations, attribution confidence, side effects and no-action counterfactual. |  | unit: A record missing any of the nine is refused and not remembered. | v11.6 p38 §24.8 What the System Learns About Agency |
| R12-AGENCY-6108 | P2 | capability | **Learn which actions work, for whom, when, with what delay** — From the memory the platform learns which actions work, for whom, under which regimes, with what delay, and when not acting is better. |  | unit: recommend picks an action where it measurably worked and none where it did not, per environment state. | v11.6 p38 §24.8 What the System Learns About Agency |
| R12-AGENCY-7027 | P2 | capability | **Action orchestrator with typed adapters, identity and durable journal** — The Causal Agency/Action Orchestrator runs as slow/warm services with typed tool adapters, a policy/identity service, causal simulation access and a durable action journal. |  | integration: An action passes through a typed adapter after policy/identity checks and is journaled durably. | v11.6 p41 §26 Platform & Deployment Architecture |
| R12-AGENCY-7028 | P0 | invariant | **Public/channel adapters use segregated credentials and conduct policy** — Public/channel adapters use segregated credentials and are subject to disclosure/conduct policy. | `EXTERNAL_ACTION` | unit: Each channel adapter is registered with its own identity; the conduct gate blocks a non-compliant communication. | v11.6 p41 §26 Platform & Deployment Architecture |
| R12-AGENCY-7029 | P0 | invariant | **No direct path from ambient model to external action** — No path exists from an ambient model to an external action without passing the agency/authority/conduct gates. |  | acceptance: Ambient types cannot reach an action adapter; advisory unwrap requires promotion. | v11.6 p41 §26 Platform & Deployment Architecture |
| R12-AGENCY-7030 | P2 | capability | **Effect attribution service with controls, multi-horizon, uncertainty** — Effect Attribution/Experiment Service supports experiment assignment where permissible, synthetic/no-action controls, multi-horizon metrics and uncertainty-aware EffectAttribution outputs. |  | unit: Attribution without a control/counterfactual method or confidence is refused. | v11.6 p41 §26 Platform & Deployment Architecture |
| R12-AGENCY-7133 | P0 | invariant | **Causal-policy drift shrinks autonomy automatically** — When observed intervention effects deviate from predicted uplift or side-effect assumptions, autonomy shrinks automatically. |  | unit: Observed effect below prediction narrows ActionPolicy authority. | v11.6 p46 §29 Autonomous Platform Operations / AIOps |
| R12-AGENCY-7148 | P0 | process | **Phase 10: causal agency begins shadow-only, then narrow reversible actions** — Phase 10 delivers GoalSpec/affordance graph, Intervention Planner, authorised adapters, conduct gate, effect attribution, no-action baselines, Action Outcome Memory and ambient-to-agency routing, beginning shadow-only and then allowing narrow reversible actions before broader authority. | `EXTERNAL_ACTION` | unit: Default authority is shadow; narrow-reversible refuses an irreversible tool. | v11.6 p48 §30 Build Order |
| R12-AGENCY-7208 | P0 | constraint | **Causal agency requires identifiable structure and measured effects** — Reliable agency requires identifiable causal structure and measured effects, not merely high predictive accuracy. |  | unit: Predictive accuracy alone does not widen authority. | v11.6 p51 §32 Technology Reality Notes (2026) |

Model and strategy foundry, training, evaluation brain.

| R12-MODEL-10123 | P1 | capability | **Resolved outcomes score forecasts, models, agents, hedges, capital and routing** — Resolved outcomes score forecasts, models, agents, hedges, capital decisions and compute-routing choices, closing the self-improvement loop. |  | integration: LEARN updates scores for each decision class. | GCP v2.1 p26 §21.5 Superintelligence Forecast-to-Capital Flow |

| R12-MODEL-10171 | P3 | process | **Build phase 12: Model Society** — Phase 12 (Model Society) delivers: specialist agent/model tournament, forecast market, adversarial verification, meeting its exit test before later phases depend on it. |  | inspection: Each listed deliverable exists and its exit test passes. | GCP v2.1 p28 §26.1 v3 Superintelligence Build Expansion |
| R12-MODEL-10180 | P0 | invariant | **Every Reflex model/policy traces to commit, manifest, signature, approval and epoch** — Every model/policy executing in Reflex can be traced to its source commit, data/evaluation manifest, signature, approval/promotion event and activation epoch. |  | unit: The activated package record carries all five links; removing one is refused. | GCP v2.1 p28 §27 Architecture Acceptance Criteria |
| R12-MODEL-10188 | P2 | invariant | **Model influence is earned from resolved performance; no permanent authority** — Model influence is earned from resolved forecast performance by domain/regime, and no single frontier model is a permanent authority. |  | unit: Weights move with resolved scores per regime; a model with poor scores loses weight. | GCP v2.1 p29 §27.1 Superintelligence Acceptance Criteria |
| R12-MODEL-10202 | P0 | invariant | **Must not promote models without replay, calibration, baseline and risk-envelope tests** — No model is promoted without replay, calibration, classical-baseline and deterministic risk-envelope compatibility tests. |  | unit: Promotion refuses a candidate missing any of the four. | GCP v2.1 p29 §28 Explicit Anti-Patterns |
| R12-MODEL-1082 | P2 | constraint | **Rust-first execution; Python/JAX/PyTorch allowed for research** — Execution is Rust-first, while research/training may use Python/JAX/PyTorch where appropriate. | `NON_RUST` `MANAGED_SERVICE` | inspection: Hot-path binaries are Rust; a research pipeline outside Rust produces artefacts consumed by Rust. | v11.6 p5 §2 Corrections Required to v10.1 |
| R12-MODEL-1083 | P1 | contract | **Export hardened inference artifacts to Rust reflex nodes** — Research-trained models are exported as hardened inference artifacts to Rust reflex nodes. |  | integration: A distilled model artifact with a content digest is accepted by the edge node only when its digest and form match; a mismatched digest is refused. | v11.6 p5 §2 Corrections Required to v10.1 |
| R12-MODEL-1084 | P1 | constraint | **Tiny reflex models on hot paths** — Hot paths keep tiny reflex models. |  | unit: Reflex model forms are bounded (linear/tree with bounded nodes) and evaluated within the pass budget. | v11.6 p5 §2 Corrections Required to v10.1 |
| R12-MODEL-1085 | P2 | capability | **Large models in slow lane, distilled into fast models** — Large multimodal/foundation/generalist models are allowed in the slow lane, with distillation into fast models. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` `COST` | integration: A slow-lane model's output is distilled into a fast student with a recorded fidelity report. | v11.6 p5 §2 Corrections Required to v10.1 |
| R12-MODEL-1157 | P2 | capability | **Model foundry capability scope** — The platform provides Model foundry with the required scope: Foundation/specialist models, supervised/unsupervised/RL, distillation, ensembles, regime models, microstructure, valuation, risk and reflex models. |  | inspection: Each scope item of 'Model foundry' maps to code reached from a composition root with a named test; a missing item fails inspection. | v11.6 p6 §3 Full Capability Map |
| R12-MODEL-1159 | P2 | capability | **Strategy factory capability scope** — The platform provides Strategy factory with the required scope: Generate, backtest, simulate, stress, paper trade, promote, decay and retire thousands of strategies. |  | inspection: Each scope item of 'Strategy factory' maps to code reached from a composition root with a named test; a missing item fails inspection. | v11.6 p6 §3 Full Capability Map |
| R12-MODEL-1198 | P1 | process | **Outcomes, episodes and missed opportunities feed the learning brain** — Outcomes, episodes and missed opportunities flow to a learning/evaluation brain that updates the world model and the tick/microstructure models. |  | integration: The LEARN stage scores declined and filled opportunities and calibrates resolved beliefs each cycle. | v11.6 p12 §5 End-to-End System Path |
| R12-MODEL-3009 | P2 | capability | **Cognitive Core trains models** — The Cognitive Core can train models from its learning data as part of the slow lane. |  | integration: A learning round produces a trained artifact with manifest; removing the training step fails. | v11.6 p15 §9 Financial Superintelligence / Cognitive Core |
| R12-MODEL-3089 | P3 | capability | **Population of thousands of specialist forecasters** — A population of thousands of specialist and temporary models independently forecast the same questions. | `COST` | inspection: Registry holds many independent forecasters for one question. | v11.6 p19 §9.8 Predictive World Swarm & Model Tournament |
| R12-MODEL-3090 | P2 | capability | **Influence earned from calibration, information value, regime accuracy, cost, latency** — Forecasters earn or lose influence from calibration, incremental information value, regime-specific accuracy, cost and latency. |  | unit: An ensemble weight moves with each of the five factors; a model with poor calibration loses weight. | v11.6 p19 §9.8 Predictive World Swarm & Model Tournament |
| R12-MODEL-3091 | P2 | capability | **Forecaster disagreement preserved as research signal** — Disagreement among forecasters is preserved and becomes a research signal. |  | unit: Material disagreement raises a research task citing the record. | v11.6 p19 §9.8 Predictive World Swarm & Model Tournament |
| R12-MODEL-3092 | P1 | metric | **Model Tournament scores by regime** — A Model Tournament continuously scores Brier, log loss, calibration, tail accuracy, economic usefulness and robustness by regime. |  | unit: Tournament output has all six metrics per model per regime; dropping one fails the schema test. | v11.6 p19 §9.8 Predictive World Swarm & Model Tournament |
| R12-MODEL-3093 | P3 | capability | **Forecast Market assigns synthetic reputation capital** — A Forecast Market gives models synthetic reputation/capital so aggregate probabilities reflect measured historical skill, not model size. |  | property: Aggregate probability weights equal reputation shares derived from scored history. | v11.6 p19 §9.8 Predictive World Swarm & Model Tournament |
| R12-MODEL-3094 | P3 | capability | **Ephemeral specialists spawned and retired** — Ephemeral specialists may be spawned for a company, election, supply shock, weather system, protocol, product or event and retired after relevance decays. |  | unit: A spawned specialist carries an expiry and is excluded once expired. | v11.6 p19 §9.8 Predictive World Swarm & Model Tournament |
| R12-MODEL-3095 | P3 | capability | **Meta-models predict forecaster failure and reweight** — Meta-models predict which forecasters are likely to fail under the current regime and dynamically adjust ensemble weights. |  | unit: Ensemble weights change on a regime change predicted to break a forecaster. | v11.6 p19 §9.8 Predictive World Swarm & Model Tournament |
| R12-MODEL-4001 | P1 | constraint | **Hot lane stays Rust-first and bounded** — Everything compiled into the hot (reflex/order) lane is Rust and has a bounded worst-case cost; no slow-lane crate (training, world model, research models) is linked into the reflex binary. |  | acceptance: A dependency-closure test of qip-edge-node finds only lane-0 crates and no slow-lane crate; adding a normal dependency from qip-edge on qip-training/qip-world-model/qip-ai must fail it. | v11.6 p21 §10 Model & Strategy Foundry |
| R12-MODEL-4002 | P0 | constraint | **Generalist models confined to the slow lane** — Foundation/generalist (large multimodal) models are used only in the slow lane for research, extraction, planning, tool use, explanation and hypothesis generation, and are never consulted on the order path or by a deterministic risk check. |  | acceptance: A test proves the hosted language-model adapter can be constructed only by the slow-lane composition root and that no reflex/fast-path crate links it; moving construction into qip-fastbrain or qip-edge-node must fail it. | v11.6 p21 §10 Model & Strategy Foundry |
| R12-MODEL-4003 | P2 | capability | **Specialist predictive models in slow/warm lanes** — The foundry provides specialist predictive models, run in slow/warm lanes, for regime, event probability, valuation, liquidity, slippage, default, volatility, demand and supply. |  | integration: Each of the nine targets has a registered, trained, evaluated model card of its class that a warm-lane consumer reads; removing one class's model leaves its consumer refusing. | v11.6 p21 §10 Model & Strategy Foundry |
| R12-MODEL-4004 | P1 | capability | **Reflex models: tiny bounded hot-lane inference** — Reflex models run in the hot lane as tiny bounded inference for order-book state, fill probability, adverse selection, short-horizon signals and opportunity scoring. |  | integration: A cell evaluates a promoted bounded model for each named purpose inside Cell::work and the result changes a decision; a model whose cost exceeds the budget is refused at install. | v11.6 p22 §10 Model & Strategy Foundry |
| R12-MODEL-4005 | P1 | invariant | **Policy/distilled models evaluated deterministically** — Policy/distilled models compressed from larger models are evaluable deterministically and cheaply in hot/warm lanes, with their fidelity to the teacher measured. |  | unit: distil returns only a measured Distillation; the distilled form evaluates bit-identically and within its cost bound; deleting the fidelity measurement makes the test fail. | v11.6 p22 §10 Model & Strategy Foundry |
| R12-MODEL-4006 | P2 | capability | **Ensembles with calibrated uncertainty and regime selection** — Ensembles (non-hot, or compact in hot) provide model diversity, calibrated uncertainty and regime-conditioned selection. |  | integration: A registered ensemble reports a calibrated predictive interval and the selector picks members by the platform's regime; a test with a regime switch changes the selection. | v11.6 p22 §10 Model & Strategy Foundry |
| R12-MODEL-4007 | P3 | capability | **Microstructure representation models** — Slow/warm-lane models learn compact representations of book, flow, liquidity and venue state from tick streams for fusion, regime detection and downstream specialist/reflex models. |  | integration: A representation model trained on tick replay emits a versioned embedding consumed by a regime detector; removing it changes the detector's inputs. | v11.6 p22 §10 Model & Strategy Foundry |
| R12-MODEL-4008 | P2 | capability | **Execution digital-twin models calibrated to telemetry** — Slow/warm execution digital-twin models of fill probability, queue, slippage, impact, latency and opportunity decay are calibrated to Algorik telemetry and historical replay. |  | integration: Twin parameters are refit from recorded fills and the calibrated twin's fill-error distribution narrows; a test shows a recalibration changing twin output. | v11.6 p22 §10 Model & Strategy Foundry |
| R12-MODEL-4009 | P2 | capability | **World-model ensembles with arbitration and disagreement** — Concurrent domain/regional/horizon/scenario world models run as an ensemble with arbitration, recorded disagreement and calibration. |  | integration: The kernel reads an arbitrated view that carries its Disagreement; two members with opposite views produce a recorded disagreement rather than an average. | v11.6 p22 §10 Model & Strategy Foundry |
| R12-MODEL-4010 | P2 | capability | **Symbolic and neuro-symbolic reasoners** — Slow/warm symbolic and neuro-symbolic reasoners provide rules, graphs, temporal/causal logic, probabilistic logic, constraints, planning and neural-symbolic hybrids. |  | integration: The REASON stage invokes rule, temporal, constraint and Bayesian reasoners and their outcome reaches the journal; a neuro-symbolic hybrid is registered and evaluated. | v11.6 p22 §10 Model & Strategy Foundry |
| R12-MODEL-4011 | P2 | capability | **Ambient forecasting/surprise models, distilled in fast lanes** — Persistent expected-state, anomaly, precursor, assumption and opportunity models run continuously, and appear in faster lanes only in distilled form. |  | integration: An ambient model emits surprise signals each cycle that reach attention/REASON; only its distilled form is linked into a fast lane. | v11.6 p22 §10 Model & Strategy Foundry |
| R12-MODEL-4012 | P2 | capability | **World-model/reflex compilers to bounded artifacts** — Compilers translate validated symbolic predicates, representations and model outputs into bounded deployable warm/reflex artifacts. |  | integration: A validated symbolic predicate compiles to a bounded program the cell installs; an unvalidated predicate is refused at compile. | v11.6 p22 §10 Model & Strategy Foundry |
| R12-MODEL-4013 | P3 | capability | **Intervention-effect/uplift models with abstention** — Uplift models estimate heterogeneous causal effects of candidate actions across entities, audiences, markets and horizons, and abstain when the effect is not identifiable. | `EXTERNAL_ACTION` | unit: An uplift estimator returns an effect with identification status and abstains on a non-identified design; a test with a confounded design asserts abstention. | v11.6 p22 §10 Model & Strategy Foundry |
| R12-MODEL-4014 | P0 | invariant | **Action-policy planners gated by simulation before authority** — Action-policy/planner models propose sequenced interventions under causal, cost, risk, legal, identity, authority and timing constraints, and are evaluated against simulators and counterfactual world-model branches before any authority is granted. | `EXTERNAL_ACTION` | integration: A plan cannot obtain execution authority unless a simulation and counterfactual-branch evaluation record is attached; removing the evaluation makes authorisation refuse. | v11.6 p22 §10 Model & Strategy Foundry |
| R12-MODEL-4015 | P1 | process | **Training data campaigns from episodes, references, licensed extracts** — A training data campaign is assembled from retained internal episodes plus reproducible external references and licensed extracts. |  | integration: A learning round's window is produced by a campaign whose manifest names each internal episode set and each external reference with licence and hash; an unadmitted source is refused at the door. | v11.6 p23 §10.1 Training Lifecycle |
| R12-MODEL-4016 | P0 | invariant | **Features computed with strict lineage and leak prevention** — Features and representations used for training are computed with strict lineage and point-in-time leak prevention. |  | integration: Every training feature carries lineage to its source records and an as-of time; the holdout gate fails a run whose feature timings leak; injecting a look-ahead feature fails it. | v11.6 p23 §10.1 Training Lifecycle |
| R12-MODEL-4017 | P0 | invariant | **Classical baseline trained first and calibrated** — Before any candidate model is evaluated, the classical baseline is trained, calibrated and on record. |  | integration: Evaluating a candidate without a calibrated baseline on record is refused; removing the calibration check lets the test's candidate through and fails it. | v11.6 p23 §10.1 Training Lifecycle |
| R12-MODEL-4019 | P1 | process | **Out-of-time validation before acceptance** — Models are validated out-of-time (holdout after the training window) before acceptance. |  | unit: The holdout is the latest slice; a fit on pure noise is refused a skill claim; changing the split to random fails the test. | v11.6 p23 §10.1 Training Lifecycle |
| R12-MODEL-4020 | P1 | process | **Cross-regime validation before acceptance** — Models are validated across regimes before acceptance; a model that has only been validated in one regime is not accepted for others. |  | integration: Acceptance requires scores in more than one classified regime, or restricts the model to the regimes it was scored in; a single-regime model used in another regime is refused. | v11.6 p23 §10.1 Training Lifecycle |
| R12-MODEL-4021 | P1 | process | **Adversarial validation before acceptance** — Models are validated adversarially (perturbed/adversarial inputs) before acceptance. |  | unit: Acceptance runs an adversarial perturbation suite and refuses a model whose output moves beyond tolerance. | v11.6 p23 §10.1 Training Lifecycle |
| R12-MODEL-4022 | P1 | process | **Simulation and paper-trading validation before acceptance** — Models are validated in simulation and paper trading before acceptance. |  | integration: A model reaches decision eligibility only after a recorded simulation/paper trial; skipping it refuses promotion. | v11.6 p23 §10.1 Training Lifecycle |
| R12-MODEL-4023 | P0 | invariant | **Accepted models signed, versioned, attested, in a Model Pack** — Every accepted model is signed, versioned, attested and added to a versioned Model Pack. |  | integration: A promotion writes a pack naming its predecessor and the model's signature; a cell refuses a model whose signature does not verify against the trust root. | v11.6 p23 §10.1 Training Lifecycle |
| R12-MODEL-4024 | P1 | capability | **Reflex models exported to bounded formats and distributed** — Reflex-compatible models are exported to bounded runtime formats and distributed to edge nodes. |  | integration: A promoted distillate appears in the trained_models manifest slot shipped over the mesh, and a cell installs a plan carrying it; a model absent from the manifest is refused. | v11.6 p23 §10.1 Training Lifecycle |
| R12-MODEL-4025 | P1 | process | **Promote/hold/retire from live, drift and counterfactual performance** — Promote, hold and retire decisions are made continuously from live (paper) performance, drift and counterfactual performance. |  | integration: A model whose drift exceeds its threshold is retired and its predecessor reactivated; a model whose counterfactual performance degrades is held; tests drive each. | v11.6 p23 §10.1 Training Lifecycle |
| R12-MODEL-6001 | P1 | invariant | **Evaluation Brain independent of the Model Foundry** — The component that judges a model or strategy (Evaluation Brain) is separate from the component that creates it (Strategy/Model Foundry), so the creator of an idea is never its sole judge. |  | inspection: Promotion decisions are taken by code with no dependency on the generator's scoring; a mutation that lets the generator's own score promote fails an architecture test. | v11.6 p32 §22 Continuous Learning & Evaluation Brain |
| R12-MODEL-6002 | P1 | capability | **Every path, including paths not taken, becomes training data** — Every decision path, including declined, unfilled and alternative paths, is captured as a learning record. |  | integration: A cycle that declines an order produces a counterfactual learning record; deleting the declined capture makes the test fail. | v11.6 p32 §22 Continuous Learning & Evaluation Brain |
| R12-MODEL-6003 | P1 | capability | **Outcome attribution by strategy, model, belief, source, policy, rule, path** — Each outcome is attributed by strategy, model, belief, evidence source, capital policy, risk rule and execution path. |  | integration: An attribution record carries all seven keys for a fill; dropping one key fails the test. | v11.6 p32 §22 Continuous Learning & Evaluation Brain |
| R12-MODEL-6004 | P1 | capability | **Counterfactual replay for declined, partial, hedged and unwound paths** — Counterfactual replay is run for declined, partially filled, hedged and unwound opportunities. |  | integration: For each of the four path kinds, LEARN produces a counterfactual set; removing one kind's queue fails its test. | v11.6 p32 §22 Continuous Learning & Evaluation Brain |
| R12-MODEL-6005 | P1 | capability | **Champion/challenger with regime-conditioned scorecards** — Models and strategies are run as champion/challenger with scorecards conditioned on regime. |  | integration: A challenger ahead in one regime and behind in another is reported split and registered only in the regime where it leads. | v11.6 p32 §22 Continuous Learning & Evaluation Brain |
| R12-MODEL-6008 | P1 | process | **Automatic retirement of degraded models and strategies** — Models and strategies that degrade are retired automatically, without a human call. |  | integration: Sustained decay at the floor retires the strategy and journals its disposition; a drifted model is retired. | v11.6 p32 §22 Continuous Learning & Evaluation Brain |
| R12-MODEL-6009 | P1 | process | **Rollback to last known-good artifact** — On retirement, the platform rolls back to the last known-good artifact. |  | integration: A degraded model's predecessor returns at the digest that passed; a rollback with no known-good predecessor is refused. | v11.6 p32 §22 Continuous Learning & Evaluation Brain |
| R12-MODEL-6016 | P1 | process | **Every forecast and model is scored after resolution** — Every forecast, world model, specialist and ensemble is scored after its outcome resolves. |  | integration: A resolved thesis is graded and charged to its component; an unresolved one is not. | v11.6 p32 §22.1 Forecast Scoreboard, Internal Prediction Market & Economic Attribution |
| R12-MODEL-6017 | P1 | metric | **Probability calibration tracked per forecaster** — The platform tracks probability calibration for each forecaster. |  | integration: A resolving cycle moves the calibration series; mutation removing the call leaves it unchanged and fails. | v11.6 p32 §22.1 Forecast Scoreboard, Internal Prediction Market & Economic Attribution |
| R12-MODEL-6018 | P1 | metric | **Directional and quantile accuracy tracked** — Directional and quantile accuracy are tracked for each forecaster. |  | unit: Quantile (pinball) loss is computed per forecaster on resolution. | v11.6 p32 §22.1 Forecast Scoreboard, Internal Prediction Market & Economic Attribution |
| R12-MODEL-6019 | P1 | metric | **Forecast tail performance tracked** — Tail performance of each forecaster is tracked. |  | unit: A forecaster that misses only tail outcomes scores worse on the tail metric than one that does not. | v11.6 p32 §22.1 Forecast Scoreboard, Internal Prediction Market & Economic Attribution |
| R12-MODEL-6020 | P2 | metric | **Incremental information value per forecaster** — The incremental information value each forecaster adds is tracked. |  | unit: A forecaster duplicating another scores near-zero incremental value. | v11.6 p32 §22.1 Forecast Scoreboard, Internal Prediction Market & Economic Attribution |
| R12-MODEL-6021 | P1 | metric | **Economic contribution and downstream capital impact per forecaster** — Economic contribution and downstream capital impact are attributed to each forecaster. |  | integration: A forecaster's attributed P&L equals the sum over the positions it informed. | v11.6 p32 §22.1 Forecast Scoreboard, Internal Prediction Market & Economic Attribution |
| R12-MODEL-6022 | P2 | capability | **Internal synthetic capital determines model influence and compute** — An internal synthetic capital/reputation ledger (internal prediction market) determines each model's future influence and compute allocation. |  | integration: A model whose synthetic capital falls loses weight in the next decision and compute share; a mutation fixing weights fails. | v11.6 p32 §22.1 Forecast Scoreboard, Internal Prediction Market & Economic Attribution |
| R12-MODEL-6074 | P1 | constraint | **New model must beat a named baseline and stay calibrated under stress** — A new model must beat a named baseline on a declared benchmark and remain calibrated under stress/regime shift. |  | integration: A candidate without a calibrated baseline on record is refused; one miscalibrated under a stressed regime is refused. | v11.6 p36 §23.4 Controlled Self-Improvement and Promotion |
| R12-MODEL-7007 | P2 | constraint | **Training/AGI runs on Google Cloud GPU/TPU compute** — Training and AGI workloads run on GPU/TPU compute on Google Cloud using fit-for-purpose tooling (Python/JAX/PyTorch or other). | `NON_RUST` `MANAGED_SERVICE` `COST` | plan: Terraform provisions an accelerator training pool; a plan without accelerators fails the check. | v11.6 p39 §26 Platform & Deployment Architecture |
| R12-MODEL-7008 | P1 | contract | **Trained artifacts export to bounded inference runtimes** — Training outputs are exported as artifacts into bounded inference runtimes rather than served from the training stack. |  | unit: A distilled artifact carries memory/latency/worst-case bounds and is refused by the reflex lane if out of bounds. | v11.6 p39 §26 Platform & Deployment Architecture |
| R12-MODEL-7022 | P2 | process | **Microstructure outputs packaged through Model Registry** — Microstructure training outputs are packaged through the Model Registry into warm/reflex-compatible artifacts. |  | integration: A trained microstructure model reaches the edge only as a registry-signed ModelPack. | v11.6 p40 §26 Platform & Deployment Architecture |
| R12-MODEL-7198 | P3 | capability | **Model Tournament and internal Forecast Market for thousands of models** — Thousands of specialist/temporary models can compete in a scored Model Tournament and internal Forecast Market. |  | acceptance: The capability runs from a composition root and a test asserts its defining property. | v11.6 p50 §31.1 v12 Superintelligence Completeness Additions |
| R12-MODEL-7201 | P3 | capability | **Adversarial verifier societies attack important predictions and models** — Adversarial verifier societies independently attack important predictions and models. |  | acceptance: The capability runs from a composition root and a test asserts its defining property. | v11.6 p50 §31.1 v12 Superintelligence Completeness Additions |
| R12-MODEL-7210 | P1 | capability | **Learn from every actual, rejected and counterfactual outcome** — The platform learns from every actual, rejected and counterfactual outcome. |  | integration: LEARN stage scores fills, declines and counterfactuals each cycle. | v11.6 p51 §33 Final Definition of Algorik |

## QUANT

Quantum Foundry and classical baselines.

| ID | P | Kind | Requirement | Flags | Verification | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R12-QUANT-9007 | P1 | constraint | **Quantum admits only asynchronous eligible research job classes** — The quantum path admits only asynchronous jobs of the eligible classes (optimisation, search, QML research) through a controlled egress gateway to IBM Quantum. | `EXTERNAL_ACTION` | unit: admit_job refuses a synchronous job and any class outside the eligible set, and the production submission path calls it; removing the call from the path fails an integration test. | GCP v2.1 p11 §11 Cognitive, Model and Quantum Compute Plane |
| R12-QUANT-9008 | P0 | invariant | **Classical benchmark and validation gate any quantum artifact promotion** — No quantum-produced artifact may be promoted unless a classical benchmark was computed on the same problem and the result validated against it. |  | integration: Each routing decision carries a finite classical objective; a quantum-gateway artifact is refused at promotion and a quantum-informed one is admitted only with its calibrated baseline. Deleting the classical solve or the refusal fails the tests. | GCP v2.1 p11 §11 Cognitive, Model and Quantum Compute Plane |
| R12-QUANT-9040 | P1 | constraint | **IBM Quantum reached only through a controlled asynchronous gateway** — IBM Quantum is reached only through a controlled, asynchronous quantum gateway. | `EXTERNAL_ACTION` `MANAGED_SERVICE` | inspection: Only the gateway identity has egress to IBM hosts and the gateway queues jobs; a second caller fails the check. | GCP v2.1 p13 §11.6 Hybrid CPU/GPU/TPU/QPU Compute Fabric |
| R12-QUANT-9041 | P3 | capability | **Compute Intelligence Router learns best substrate per problem class** — A Compute Intelligence Router learns which substrate (CPU, GPU, TPU, quantum-inspired, QPU) gives the best verified result for each problem class. | `COST` | unit: Recorded verified outcomes per problem class change the router's substrate choice. | GCP v2.1 p13 §11.6 Hybrid CPU/GPU/TPU/QPU Compute Fabric |
| R12-QUANT-9042 | P1 | constraint | **CPU/Rust pools own deterministic compute as low-cost baseline** — CPU/Rust pools on Compute Engine own symbolic solvers, graph algorithms, deterministic services and Fabric/Reflex tooling, scaled by fixed reservations as the low-cost baseline. | `KUBERNETES` | plan: Execution nodes consume a specific reservation sized to target; deterministic services are Rust on CPU. | GCP v2.1 p13 §11.6 Hybrid CPU/GPU/TPU/QPU Compute Fabric |
| R12-QUANT-9043 | P3 | capability | **GPU substrate for training, multimodal inference, simulation** — GPU pools (Vertex custom training) serve foundation/specialist training, multimodal inference and simulation, with Spot for interruptible jobs. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` `KUBERNETES` `COST` | plan: A training job spec names a GPU machine type and Spot for interruptible runs. | GCP v2.1 p13 §11.6 Hybrid CPU/GPU/TPU/QPU Compute Fabric |

| R12-QUANT-9045 | P0 | invariant | **Quantum-inspired classical baseline always available before any QPU claim** — Quantum-inspired classical heuristics (QUBO/Ising, annealing-style) are always available as the baseline before any QPU claim. |  | unit: SolverBenchmark always includes a classical solver record and refuses a candidate failing classical validation. | GCP v2.1 p13 §11.6 Hybrid CPU/GPU/TPU/QPU Compute Fabric |
| R12-QUANT-9046 | P1 | constraint | **QPU work budgeted, off the hot path, classically verified** — QPU work runs in a budgeted queue, with no hot-path dependency and mandatory classical verification. | `COST` `EXTERNAL_ACTION` | unit: Jobs over budget are refused; no execution crate reaches a solver; every QPU result is validated classically. | GCP v2.1 p13 §11.6 Hybrid CPU/GPU/TPU/QPU Compute Fabric |
| R12-QUANT-10066 | P3 | constraint | **TPU7x only for workloads with measured framework/topology fit** — TPU7x (Ironwood) is used only for training/inference whose JAX/PyTorch and topology benefit is measured, never assumed for all workloads. | `COST` `MANAGED_SERVICE` | unit: The compute router admits a TPU rung only with a recorded benchmark showing benefit. | GCP v2.1 p22 §19.0B Superintelligence & Compute; GCP v2.1 p30 §29.1 Current Capability Validation - v3 Additions (2026); GCP v2.1 p29 §27.1 Superintelligence Acceptance Criteria |

| R12-QUANT-1011 | P1 | invariant | **Quantum used only where measurable advantage exists** — Quantum is a hybrid accelerator used only where measurable advantage against a classical baseline exists. |  | unit: Every cycle that sizes a proposal journals the classical baseline and the improvement; a benchmark whose classical baseline fails reports nothing. | v11.6 p2 §1 Executive Summary |
| R12-QUANT-1012 | P0 | invariant | **Quantum never confers truth or bypasses controls** — Quantum output never confers truth and never bypasses evidence requirements or deterministic controls; it is advisory and classically verified. |  | unit: Quantum results are typed as advisory candidates that must be validated against the request and the classical objective before use; an unvalidated result is refused. | v11.6 p2 §1 Executive Summary; v11.6 p6 §2 Corrections Required to v10.1; v11.6 p10 §4 Time-Lane Architecture |

| R12-QUANT-1080 | P3 | capability | **Quantum Foundry supports quantum reservoir/extreme-learning research** — The Quantum Foundry supports quantum reservoir/extreme-learning research as a benchmarked workload family. |  | unit: The foundry registers the ReservoirAndExtremeLearning family with an encoding, classical baseline and benchmark; status is not 'not built'. | v11.6 p4 §2 Corrections Required to v10.1 |

| R12-QUANT-1145 | P3 | capability | **Quantum research for symbolic search, ensembles, hypothesis space** — Quantum research covers selected symbolic/combinatorial search, world-model/scenario ensemble selection and hypothesis-space exploration. |  | unit: Families SymbolicCombinatorialSearch and ScenarioHypothesisSelection are built with baselines. | v11.6 p6 §2 Corrections Required to v10.1 |

| R12-QUANT-1193 | P0 | invariant | **Lane 4 quantum/research never blocks execution** — Lane 4 quantum-classical and research workloads are asynchronous and never block execution. |  | unit: Synchronous quantum jobs are refused at admission, and the decision path completes with the quantum provider unavailable. | v11.6 p10 §4 Time-Lane Architecture |
| R12-QUANT-1194 | P0 | invariant | **Quantum output advisory and classically verified** — Quantum output is advisory and classically verified before use. |  | unit: Every quantum answer is re-scored on the classical objective and only used if better by the threshold. | v11.6 p10 §4 Time-Lane Architecture |
| R12-QUANT-4018 | P3 | constraint | **Quantum-assisted model variants only on eligible workloads** — Quantum-assisted model variants are optional and evaluated only on eligible workloads, against the calibrated classical baseline. |  | integration: A quantum-informed candidate is admitted only with a calibrated baseline reference and an eligible workload family; an ineligible family is refused. | v11.6 p23 §10.1 Training Lifecycle |
| R12-QUANT-4026 | P0 | invariant | **Every quantum workload has a classical baseline** — Every quantum workload has a classical baseline solved on the same problem every time the quantum path runs. |  | integration: Every routing decision carries a classical objective; a workload family without a baseline is refused registration; removing the classical solve fails the router test. | v11.6 p23 §11 Quantum Foundry |
| R12-QUANT-4027 | P0 | invariant | **Quantum and classical scored on a common objective** — Each quantum workload declares one common objective on which both quantum and classical paths are scored. |  | unit: Every SolverRun's objective comes from the same PortfolioProblem::objective_at; a test with a solver reporting its own objective is corrected or refused. | v11.6 p23 §11 Quantum Foundry |
| R12-QUANT-4028 | P0 | invariant | **Quantum promotion threshold per workload** — Each quantum workload has a stated, positive promotion threshold the quantum result must beat the classical baseline by; ties go to classical. |  | unit: A quantum answer better by less than the margin loses; a tie loses; setting the margin to zero fails the tie test. | v11.6 p23 §11 Quantum Foundry |
| R12-QUANT-4029 | P1 | constraint | **QPU jobs are asynchronous** — QPU jobs are submitted asynchronously; no caller blocks on a quantum queue for an answer it needs inline. |  | integration: The only path that reaches a provider submits a job and returns; a synchronous submission is refused; a test with a slow provider shows the cycle completing on the classical answer. | v11.6 p23 §11 Quantum Foundry |
| R12-QUANT-4030 | P0 | invariant | **QPU never on the market hot path** — No quantum or QPU code is linked into, or called from, the market hot path. |  | acceptance: qip-edge-node's dependency closure excludes qip-quantum and qip-optimization-engine; adding the dependency fails the lane test. | v11.6 p23 §11 Quantum Foundry |
| R12-QUANT-4031 | P0 | invariant | **Quantum output is a candidate; classical verification is authoritative** — Quantum output is treated only as a candidate solution or search aid, never proof of truth; classical verification and evidence remain authoritative. |  | unit: A quantum result is usable only after classical recomputation and feasibility check; a provider claim that disagrees with recomputation is refused; a quantum-gateway output offered as a model candidate is refused. | v11.6 p23 §11 Quantum Foundry |
| R12-QUANT-4032 | P2 | capability | **Portfolio/family allocation via QUBO/QAOA** — Portfolio/family allocation can be formulated as QUBO and solved by QAOA or another hybrid optimizer under cardinality, diversification and exposure constraints, against the classical baseline. |  | integration: With quantum enabled and an eligible problem, the kernel attempts QAOA, and the routing journal records both runs; constraint-violating quantum answers are refused. | v11.6 p23 §11 Quantum Foundry |
| R12-QUANT-4033 | P3 | capability | **Multi-leg path selection as QUBO/graph optimisation** — Multi-leg cycle/path sets can be selected by QUBO/graph optimisation under inventory, settlement, capital and concurrency constraints, against a classical solver. |  | unit: A path-selection QUBO decodes to a feasible cycle set and is benchmarked against the classical path router. | v11.6 p23 §11 Quantum Foundry |
| R12-QUANT-4034 | P3 | capability | **Capital placement as combinatorial optimisation** — Where collateral and cash sit across regions/venues is posed as a combinatorial optimisation (quantum-eligible) given expected opportunities and transfer cost, with a classical baseline. |  | unit: A placement problem has a QUBO formulation benchmarked against the classical pre-positioning planner. | v11.6 p23 §11 Quantum Foundry |
| R12-QUANT-4035 | P3 | capability | **Feature/ensemble selection by quantum-assisted binary selection** — Compact learner/feature sets for specialist or reflex models can be chosen by quantum-assisted binary selection, against a classical selector. |  | unit: A feature-selection QUBO with a classical baseline selector, scored on held-out loss. | v11.6 p23 §11 Quantum Foundry |
| R12-QUANT-4036 | P3 | capability | **Quantum kernels / variational models research** — Quantum kernels and variational circuits are evaluated for whether they add measurable classification/regression value over classical models. | `NEW_DEPENDENCY` | unit: A kernel/variational experiment reports out-of-sample uplift versus a strong classical model. | v11.6 p23 §11 Quantum Foundry |
| R12-QUANT-4037 | P3 | capability | **Quantum reservoir / extreme learning research** — Quantum reservoir/extreme-learning paths are evaluated experimentally for selected time-series or nonlinear tasks. |  | unit: A reservoir experiment with a classical reservoir baseline on the same task. | v11.6 p23 §11 Quantum Foundry |
| R12-QUANT-4038 | P3 | capability | **Scenario/hypothesis selection under compute budgets** — Diverse high-information scenarios/experiments are selected by optimisation under limited compute/data budgets. |  | unit: A selection formulation chooses a scenario subset under a budget and is compared with a classical greedy selector. | v11.6 p23 §11 Quantum Foundry |
| R12-QUANT-4039 | P3 | constraint | **Evidence graph clustering, never a truth oracle** — Quantum-assisted evidence-graph clustering/selection is experimental and its output is never used as a truth oracle. |  | unit: Clustering output enters only as a candidate grouping and cannot set a belief's truth value. | v11.6 p24 §11 Quantum Foundry |
| R12-QUANT-4040 | P3 | capability | **Symbolic/combinatorial reasoning search compared to classical solvers** — Quantum QUBO/QAOA search over constraint solutions, graph selections or symbolic hypothesis subsets is used only where the formulation is suitable and is compared to classical solvers. |  | unit: A constraint problem's QUBO decodes to the classical minimiser's optimum on test instances and is benchmarked against it. | v11.6 p24 §11 Quantum Foundry |
| R12-QUANT-4041 | P3 | capability | **World-model ensemble selection preserving minority hypotheses** — Model/scenario subsets are selected under compute budgets while preserving uncertainty and minority hypotheses. |  | unit: A selection that drops all minority hypotheses is refused. | v11.6 p24 §11 Quantum Foundry |
| R12-QUANT-4042 | P3 | constraint | **Hypothesis-space exploration is research, never validation** — Quantum-assisted exploration of hypothesis spaces is research only and is never treated as empirical validation. |  | unit: A hypothesis whose only support is a quantum exploration result cannot reach a validated state. | v11.6 p24 §11 Quantum Foundry |
| R12-QUANT-4043 | P1 | invariant | **Intervention plans re-scored classically before execution** — Any candidate intervention portfolio/sequence produced by combinatorial (quantum) search is re-scored by classical causal models, policy gates and simulation before any execution. | `EXTERNAL_ACTION` | integration: A searched plan cannot be executed without a classical causal score, a policy-gate pass and a simulation record. | v11.6 p24 §11 Quantum Foundry |
| R12-QUANT-4044 | P3 | constraint | **Counterfactual branch selection is not causal proof** — Quantum-assisted selection of counterfactual/intervention branches for expensive simulation is a selection aid only; no quantum result is treated as causal proof. |  | unit: A causal claim cannot cite a quantum branch-selection result as evidence. | v11.6 p24 §11 Quantum Foundry |
| R12-QUANT-4045 | P0 | invariant | **Reality boundary: classical ships unless quantum wins on objective, cost and latency** — Production use of a quantum result requires benchmarked improvement over classical methods on the same objective, cost and latency constraints; if quantum loses, the classical result ships. |  | unit: A quantum answer that wins on objective but exceeds the cost or latency budget is not used; a losing quantum answer leaves the classical result in force. | v11.6 p24 §11 Quantum Foundry |
| R12-QUANT-4046 | P2 | capability | **Problem compiler routes across compute substrates** — A Problem Compiler and Compute Intelligence Router decide whether work runs on Rust/CPU, GPU, TPU, quantum-inspired algorithms, IBM QPU hardware or parallel ensembles. | `MANAGED_SERVICE` `COST` | integration: A routed problem records which substrate was chosen among the declared set and why. | v11.6 p24 §11.1 Quantum-Classical Cognitive Compute Fabric |
| R12-QUANT-4047 | P2 | capability | **Routing policy learns from measured outcomes** — No substrate is presumed superior; routing policy is learned from measured quality, calibration, latency, repeatability and cost. |  | integration: Recorded benchmarks update the routing policy; a substrate that consistently loses stops being tried. | v11.6 p24 §11.1 Quantum-Classical Cognitive Compute Fabric |
| R12-QUANT-4048 | P2 | capability | **Classify the task before routing** — The compiler classifies each task as forecasting, graph search, combinatorial optimisation, sampling, kernel method, simulation, symbolic solving or model training. |  | unit: Each of the eight classes is a variant; an unclassified task is refused. | v11.6 p25 §11.2 Problem Compiler & Hybrid Compute Router |
| R12-QUANT-4049 | P2 | capability | **Extract variables, constraints, objective, tolerances, deadlines, proofs** — The compiler extracts variables, constraints, objective, tolerances, deadlines and required proof/verification properties for each task. |  | unit: A compiled problem carries all six; a missing one is refused rather than defaulted. | v11.6 p25 §11.2 Problem Compiler & Hybrid Compute Router |
| R12-QUANT-4050 | P1 | capability | **Generate classical baseline and eligible quantum formulations** — For each task the compiler generates a classical baseline and eligible quantum/quantum-inspired formulations (QUBO/Ising, kernels, variational circuits). |  | unit: A compiled task has a classical formulation always and quantum formulations only where eligible. | v11.6 p25 §11.2 Problem Compiler & Hybrid Compute Router |
| R12-QUANT-4051 | P2 | capability | **Parallel dispatch when expected information value justifies** — Candidates are dispatched to one or more substrates in parallel only when expected information value justifies the cost. | `COST` | unit: Dispatch records an expected-information-value estimate and declines dispatch below it. | v11.6 p25 §11.2 Problem Compiler & Hybrid Compute Router |
| R12-QUANT-4052 | P0 | invariant | **Verify returned candidates classically** — Every returned candidate is verified classically before use. |  | unit: A candidate whose recomputed objective or feasibility disagrees is refused. | v11.6 p25 §11.2 Problem Compiler & Hybrid Compute Router |
| R12-QUANT-4053 | P1 | process | **Record quality/cost/latency and update Solver Registry** — Each routed solve records quality, cost and latency and updates the Solver Registry and future routing policy. |  | integration: The journal record carries quality, cost and latency per solver run, and the registry's view of a solver changes after it. | v11.6 p25 §11.2 Problem Compiler & Hybrid Compute Router |
| R12-QUANT-4054 | P3 | invariant | **Forecasting quantum research checked out-of-time vs strong classical** — Quantum kernels/reservoir/QML or ensemble feature spaces used for forecasting are checked by out-of-time calibration against strong classical models. |  | unit: A quantum forecasting experiment reports out-of-time calibration beside a strong classical model's. | v11.6 p25 §11.3 Expanded Quantum Research Surface |
| R12-QUANT-4055 | P3 | invariant | **World-model quantum search checked for coherence and reproducibility** — Quantum hypothesis-subset/scenario selection or graph partition for world models is checked for causal/evidence coherence and reproducibility. |  | unit: Selected subsets are re-run for reproducibility and checked for causal/evidence coherence. | v11.6 p25 §11.3 Expanded Quantum Research Surface |
| R12-QUANT-4056 | P3 | invariant | **Hedging quantum search checked on constraints, Greeks, liquidity, cost** — Quantum hedge-basket and tail-scenario optimisation is checked classically for constraints, Greeks, liquidity and executable cost. |  | unit: A quantum hedge basket violating a Greek or liquidity bound is refused. | v11.6 p25 §11.3 Expanded Quantum Research Surface |
| R12-QUANT-4057 | P3 | invariant | **Capital quantum search checked on invariants and transaction costs** — Quantum portfolio, collateral, funding and capital-placement search is checked against risk/capital invariants and transaction costs. |  | unit: A quantum allocation that breaks a capital invariant or ignores transaction cost is refused. | v11.6 p25 §11.3 Expanded Quantum Research Surface |
| R12-QUANT-4058 | P3 | invariant | **Arbitrage quantum search checked on executable edge and saga feasibility** — Quantum multi-leg path/cycle and reservation optimisation is checked for live executable edge and bounded-loss saga feasibility. |  | unit: A selected cycle whose executable edge is negative or saga infeasible is refused. | v11.6 p25 §11.3 Expanded Quantum Research Surface |
| R12-QUANT-4059 | P3 | invariant | **Model-society quantum selection checked on score uplift and economics** — Quantum feature/ensemble/agent-team selection is checked for forecast score uplift and compute economics. |  | unit: A selection reports score uplift and compute cost against the classical selection. | v11.6 p25 §11.3 Expanded Quantum Research Surface |
| R12-QUANT-4060 | P3 | invariant | **Simulation quantum sampling checked by verification and bias analysis** — Quantum sampling and combinatorial state selection for simulation is checked by classical verification and bias analysis. |  | unit: Sampled states are classically verified and a bias analysis is recorded. | v11.6 p25 §11.3 Expanded Quantum Research Surface |
| R12-QUANT-4061 | P1 | invariant | **Quantum outputs enter as ensemble features, not authority** — Classical, quantum-inspired and QPU methods produce independent candidates whose outputs become ensemble features rather than direct authority. |  | integration: A quantum-derived output reaches a decision only as an input feature to an evaluated model; offering it as a model is refused. | v11.6 p25 §11.4 Quantum-Classical Ensemble Learning |
| R12-QUANT-4062 | P3 | capability | **Evaluation learns where quantum representations add information** — The Evaluation Brain learns, per regime, whether a quantum-derived representation adds information beyond classical baselines. |  | integration: A per-regime information-gain score is recorded for each quantum-derived feature. | v11.6 p25 §11.4 Quantum-Classical Ensemble Learning |
| R12-QUANT-4063 | P3 | constraint | **QPU work stays asynchronous research behind stable contracts** — Today QPU jobs remain asynchronous research workloads, and the cognitive contracts are shaped so that deeper subproblems for future fault-tolerant hardware need no contract redesign. |  | inspection: The quantum request/result contract is solver-agnostic (QUBO + result + baseline) and no research job class reaches production authority. | v11.6 p25 §11.5 Hardware Evolution Boundary |
| R12-QUANT-4064 | P0 | invariant | **Quantum never establishes truth or bypasses deterministic controls** — No quantum result establishes truth or bypasses deterministic financial controls; anything sized from a quantum answer still passes the deterministic pre-trade gate. |  | integration: A proposal sized by a winning quantum answer reaches the OMS and is refused by PreTradeChecker when it breaches a limit. | v11.6 p25 §11.5 Hardware Evolution Boundary |
| R12-QUANT-6007 | P1 | metric | **Quantum-vs-classical benchmark per workload family and instance size** — Each quantum-vs-classical comparison is recorded keyed by workload family and instance size. |  | integration: A cycle that sizes a proposal publishes one SolverBenchmarked record keyed by family and size matching its journal entry. | v11.6 p32 §22 Continuous Learning & Evaluation Brain |
| R12-QUANT-7016 | P2 | capability | **Isolated Quantum Gateway with queue-aware async jobs and fallback** — IBM Quantum is reached only through an isolated Quantum Gateway/Foundry with queue-aware asynchronous job orchestration and a classical fallback. | `EXTERNAL_ACTION` | unit: A synchronous job is refused; a queued job that times out yields the classical answer. | v11.6 p40 §26 Platform & Deployment Architecture |
| R12-QUANT-7144 | P3 | process | **Phase 6: classical benchmark first, ship only measured quantum wins** — Phase 6 builds the classical benchmark first, then quantum optimisation and QML plug-ins, shipping only measured wins, including benchmarked symbolic/combinatorial and scenario-ensemble experiments. |  | inspection: Every named exit capability exists and is tested. | v11.6 p47 §30 Build Order |
| R12-QUANT-7206 | P1 | invariant | **Quantum is async, benchmarked, never assumed advantage nor hot-path dependency** — Quantum is a native asynchronous substrate inside a benchmarked hybrid compute fabric, never an assumed advantage and never a hot-path dependency. |  | acceptance: Synchronous quantum jobs are refused, every quantum answer is compared with a classical baseline, and reflex crates do not depend on qip-quantum. | v11.6 p50 §31.1 v12 Superintelligence Completeness Additions |
| R12-QUANT-7207 | P1 | constraint | **Quantum Foundry is operationally benchmark-gated** — The Quantum Foundry may be architecturally broad but is operationally benchmark-gated: nothing ships without a measured benefit against the classical baseline. |  | unit: A quantum answer that ties the classical baseline loses. | v11.6 p51 §32 Technology Reality Notes (2026) |
| R12-QUANT-7209 | P0 | invariant | **Quantum search never establishes causal truth or authorises interventions** — Quantum search may explore candidate action/scenario spaces but cannot establish causal truth or authorise real-world interventions. |  | acceptance: No quantum crate is a dependency of the agency authorisation path. | v11.6 p51 §32 Technology Reality Notes (2026) |
